\rho\coloneq(s_{0},\beta^{!}_{0},\beta^{?}_{0})\xrightarrow{u_{0}}(s_{1},\beta^{!}_{1},\beta^{?}_{1})\xrightarrow{u_{1}}\ldots

in
M
for

u\coloneq u_{0}\cdot u_{1}\cdot\ldots
.
We claim the following:
for every
i

with

(s_{i},\beta^{!}_{i},\beta^{?}_{i})\xrightarrow{u_{i}}(s_{i+1},\beta^{!}_{i+1},\beta^{?}_{i+1})
,
it holds that

s_{i}\xrightarrow{\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(u_{i})}s_{i+1}
.
Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
By construction of
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
, there is

s_{i}\xrightarrow{x}s_{i+1}
such that

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(x)=u_{i}
.
We apply
\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\hbox{-})
on both sides and obtain

\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(x))=\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(u_{i})
.
By

(1)
,
it get
x=\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(u_{i})
, which proves the claim.
Together, this shows that there is a run with trace
\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(u)
in
M
.

With
u\sim u^{\prime}
and

(6)

we have that

\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(u)\sim\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(u^{\prime})
.
Thus, we have
\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(u)\in\mathcal{S}(M)
, which concludes this proof.

•

(8)
:
The direction from left to right was proven as part of the proof for
(7)
.
The direction from right to left follows from

(7)

if one instantiates
w=\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)
:
if
\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)\in\mathcal{S}(M)
then

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w))\in\mathcal{S}(\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M))
;
with

(2)
, we obtain that

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w))=w
, proving the claim.

This concludes all proofs.

\square

Lemma 6

Let
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
be a CSM.
Then, for every
w\in\Gamma^{\infty}
that respects
\beta
, it holds that

w\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})
iff

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}})
.

Proof

We show that the run for
w
in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}

can be simulated
for
\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)
in

\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
,
and vice versa.

Prior, we establish some notation that will be used for both cases.

Let
w\coloneq w_{0}\cdot w_{1}\ldots
and

\rho
be the run in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
for
w
:

(\vec{q}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{w_{0}}(\vec{q}_{1},\xi_{1})\xrightarrow{w_{1}}\ldots
.
For

\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
,
we split the states of participants as follows:
for participants from
\mathcal{P}
, we have
\vec{s}_{i}
as well as
\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}}
and
\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}}
for the respective channel bounds;
for participants from
\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{Ch}}
, we have
\vec{t}_{i}
.
Then, let
\rho^{\prime}
be the run in

\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}

for
\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)
:

(\vec{s}_{0},\vec{\beta^{!}_{0}},\vec{\beta^{?}_{0}},\vec{t}_{0},\xi^{\prime}_{0})\xrightarrow{\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{0})}(\vec{s}_{1},\vec{\beta^{!}_{1}},\vec{\beta^{?}_{1}},\vec{t}_{1},\xi^{\prime}_{1})\xrightarrow{\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{1})}\ldots\kern 5.0pt.

Intuitively, the channel participants store the channel content while the actual channels are empty.
More precisely,
We introduce notation for this.
Intuitively,
\xi(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
contains what is stored in states

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}
for
\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{i}}}
between
from

\beta^{?}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
to

\beta^{!}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
(excl.)
and all the other channel participants, i.e.
\beta^{!}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
to

\beta^{?}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
(excl.)
are in their initial state;
where we consider the indices for channel participants to form a ring buffer, e.g. ,

5
to
2
(excl.) gives the sequence
5,6,0,1
if the channel bound is
7
.

Formally, we define

\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t},\vec{\beta^{!}},\vec{\beta^{?}},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)\coloneq w_{i}\ldots w_{j}

for the states
\vec{t}
of channel participants if the following holds:

•

j=\vec{\beta^{!}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
,

•

i=\vec{\beta^{?}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
,

•

for every
i\leq k<j
, it holds that
\vec{t}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{k}}}}}}=q_{m}
and
w_{k}=m
, and

•

for every
j\leq k<i
, it holds that
\vec{t}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{k}}}}}}=q_{0,f}
.

First, let us assume there exists run
\rho
for
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
for
w=w_{0}\cdot w_{1}\cdot\ldots
.
We claim the following:
for every
i

with

(\vec{q}_{i},\xi_{i})\xrightarrow{w_{i}}(\vec{q}_{i+1},\xi_{i+1})
and

(\vec{s}_{i},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\vec{t}_{i},\xi^{\prime}_{i})

such that

(a)

\vec{q}_{i}=\vec{s}_{i}
,

(b)

\xi^{\prime}_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(c)

\xi^{\prime}_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\varepsilon

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(d)

\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

there is

(\vec{s}_{i+1},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i+1}},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i+1}},\vec{t}_{i+1},\xi^{\prime}_{i+1})

such that

(a’)

\vec{q}_{i+1}=\vec{s}_{i+1}
,

(b’)

\xi^{\prime}_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(c’)

\xi^{\prime}_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\varepsilon

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(d’)

\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i+1},\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
, and

(e’)

(\vec{s}_{i},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\vec{t}_{i},\xi^{\prime}_{i})\xrightarrow{\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})}\!\!^{2}\,(\vec{s}_{i+1},\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\vec{\beta^{?}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\vec{t}_{i+1},\xi^{\prime}_{i+1})
.

With the above claim, we need one more ingredient to show that the run for
w
can be mimicked with one for
\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)
:
we need to relate the initial states.
Concretely, we show that

(a)

to

(d)

hold for
i=0
.
In fact, all of these conditions are trivial: initially, all participants are in their initial state and all channels are empty.

Having related the initial states, it is straightforward that the above claim can be used to mimic the run for
w
in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
in

\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
, having trace
\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)
.
Intuitively, we start with the initial states and, for every
w_{i}
, we apply the above claim to obtain the mimicked run.

It remains to prove the above claim.
Let
i
be some number.
We assume that

(a)

to

(d)

hold.
We show that

(a’)

to

(e’)

hold.
We use
(e’)
to obtain the witness

(\vec{s}_{i+1},\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\vec{\beta^{?}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\vec{t}_{i+1},\xi^{\prime}_{i+1})
.
We will still need to show that this transition is possible.
We do a simultaneous case analysis whether
w_{i}
is a send or receive transition and whether the corresponding channel is in the domain of
\beta
.

•

w_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
and

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
:

Because of
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,
we have that

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})=w_{i}
.
From
(a)
, we know that
\vec{q}_{i}
and
\vec{s}_{i}
agree on the state for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
, which is the active participant for
w_{i}
.
Therefore, the send transition for

(e’)

is possible.
All claims

(a’)

to

(d’)

trivially follow by the semantics of CSMs and the fact that channel participants are not involved for transitions for which the corresponding channel is not in the domain of
\beta
.

•

w_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m
and

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
:

From
(a)
, we know that
\vec{q}_{i}
and
\vec{s}_{i}
agree on the state for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
, which is the active participant for
w_{i}
.
In addition, we know that the channel content for
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle
are the same in

\xi_{i}
and
\xi^{\prime}_{i}
from

(b)
.
Therefore, the receive transition for

(e’)

is possible.
Again, the remaining claims

(a’)

to

(d’)

trivially follow by the semantics of CSMs and the fact that channel participants are not involved for transitions for which the corresponding channel is not in the domain of
\beta
.

•

w_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
and

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
:

We have that

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{\to}\kern 0.39993pt{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{:}\kern 0.39993ptm

where

\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{j}}}\equiv\lvert(w_{1}\ldots w_{i-1}){\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!\_}\rvert

by construction.
We first argue that the transition in

(e’)

is possible.
In fact, it contains two transitions: one send for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
and one receive for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}
.
Because of

(a)
,
the states for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
in
\vec{q}_{i}
and
\vec{s}_{i}
agree and, thus, the transition is possible.
After this transition, message
m
is in the channel from
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
to
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}
and, by

(c)
, this is the only message.
From

(d)
,
we also know that this channel participant is in its initial state in
\vec{t}_{i+1}
.
Thus, it can receive
m
by construction, with which it updates its state to
q_{m}
.
Therefore, this channel is empty again and, thus,
(c’)
holds.
After the first send transition,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
is in the same state and thus

(a’)
is satisfied by determinism. None of the other participants change their state and thus
(b’)
is (still) satisfied.
There are only changes related
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle
.
Thus, we only need to consider these changes for
(d’)
.

By the semantics of CSMs, we have

\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)\cdot m
.
We also have

(\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1})_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}=(\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}})_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

for all
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.

Thus, it remains to show

\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)\cdot m=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i+1},\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
.

From

\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

to

\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i+1},\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
,
there are only the following changes:
the state change of

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}

in
\vec{t}_{i+1}

and the increase of

(\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1})_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=(\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}})_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)+1
,
while

(\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pt{}_{i}+1})_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}=(\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}})_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

for all
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
It is easy to show that these add precisely
m
to

\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i+1},\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

to

\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

which proves the claim.

•

w_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m
and

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
:

We have that

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{\to}\kern 0.39993pt{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{:}\kern 0.39993ptm

where

\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{j}}}\equiv\lvert(w_{1}\ldots w_{i-1}){\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?\_}\rvert

by construction.
We first argue that the transition in

(e’)

is possible.
In fact, it contains two transitions:
one send for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}
and
one receive for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
.
Regarding the second transition:
because of

(a)
,
the states for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
in
\vec{q}_{i}
and
\vec{s}_{i}
agree and, thus, the transition is possible.
Regarding the first transition:
since the previous transition is possible in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
, we know that
m
is at the head of the channel

\xi(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
;
from
(d)
, we know that the state of

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}
is
q_{m}
in
\vec{t}_{i}
, making the first transition possible and putting it back to its initial state.
As for the other cases, it is straightforward that

(a’)

to

(c’)

are satisfied after these transitions.

It remains to show

(d’)
.
There are only changes related
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle
.
Thus, we only need to consider these changes.

By the semantics of CSMs, we have

\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=m\cdot\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
.
We also have

(\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1})_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}=(\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}})_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

for all
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.

The only changes
from

\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

to

\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i+1},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}},\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

are: the state change of

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}

in
\vec{t}_{i+1}

and the increase of

(\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1})_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=(\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}})_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)+1
,
while

(\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1})_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}=(\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}})_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

for all
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
It is easy to show that these remove precisely
m
,
which proves the claim.

This concludes the proof for this direction.

Second, let us assume there exists run
\rho
for

\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}

for
\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)
.
We claim the following:
for every
i

with

(\vec{s}_{i},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\vec{t}_{i},\xi^{\prime}_{i})\xrightarrow{\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})}\!\!^{2}\,(\vec{s}_{i+1},\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\vec{\beta^{!}}_{\kern-2.45836pti+1},\vec{t}_{i+1},\xi^{\prime}_{i+1})

and

(\vec{q}_{i},\xi_{i})

such that

(a)

\vec{q}_{i}=\vec{s}_{i}
,

(b)

\xi^{\prime}_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(c)

\xi^{\prime}_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\varepsilon

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(d)

\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i}},\vec{\beta^{?}_{i}},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

there is

(\vec{q}_{i+1},\xi_{i+1})

such that

(a’)

\vec{q}_{i+1}=\vec{s}_{i+1}
,

(b’)

\xi^{\prime}_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(c’)

\xi^{\prime}_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\varepsilon

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(d’)

\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i+1},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i+1}},\vec{\beta^{!}_{i+1}},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
, and

(e’)

(\vec{q}_{i},\xi_{i})\xrightarrow{w_{i}}(\vec{q}_{i+1},\xi_{i+1})
.

As before, we need one more ingredient to show that one run simulates the other: the conditions hold for the initial states, i.e.
i=0
.
Again, this is trivially true.
In fact, one can also prove the above claim analogously so we omit the full proof.

We want the channel participants only to faithfully forward messages.
To formalise this, we define the notions of
forwarding
and
almost forwarding
.

Definition 19
(Forwarding and almost forwarding)

A word
w=w_{1}\ldots\in(\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}})^{\infty}

if
forwarding
is for every odd
j
, it holds that

w_{j}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m

and

w_{j+1}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
.
If
w=w_{1}\ldots w_{n}
is finite, it is
almost forwarding
if
w_{1}\ldots w_{n-1}
is forwarding and

w_{n}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m
.
A language
L\subseteq(\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}})^{\infty}
is forwarding if for every word is.
We say that an FSM
A
over alphabet
\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}
is
forwarding
if its language
\mathcal{L}(A)
is forwarding.

Intuitively, for finite words, we only want forwarding words.
For infinite words, though, it can happen that some send events will never be matched.
Because of the channel bounds, this, however, cannot happen indefinitely, which is why we use the notion of almost forwarding for infinite words whose projection onto channel participants will be finite.

Lemma 7

Let
M
be a PSM that respects channel bounds
\beta
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}
be a channel participant.
Then, the following holds
for every
u\in\mathcal{S}(\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M))
:

•

If
u
is finite, then
w=u{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma^{\mathsf{wCh}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}}
is forwarding.

•

If
u
is infinite, then
w=u{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma^{\mathsf{wCh}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}}
is almost forwarding or forwarding.

Proof

Let
u\in\mathcal{S}(\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M))
be a word.
We do a case distinction if
u
is finite of infinite.

Suppose
u
is finite.
Then, we have to show that

w=u{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma^{\mathsf{wCh}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}}
is forwarding.
By assumption, we know that
w
respects
\beta
.
We show that
w
is forwarding with reasoning from
Lemma

4
.
From Claim 1 in

Lemma

4
,
we know the following:
for every
0\leq j<\beta(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
, it holds that

h(w,j,\beta(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle))\in\{x\cdot y\mid x\in\Gamma_{{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle},!}\text{ and }y\in\Gamma_{{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle},?}\}^{*}\kern 5.0pt.

Later in that lemma, we have drawn the connection between

h(w,j,\beta(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle))

and

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w){\Downarrow}_{\Gamma^{\mathsf{wCh}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}}
.
In particular, they consider the same pattern to keep letters:
starting from the
j
-th occurrence,
it turns
every
\beta(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
-th instance of
a send event

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!\_

turns into

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}!\_

and
every
\beta(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
-th instance of
a receive event

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?\_

turns into

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}?\_
.
By construction and the fact that
w
is FIFO-compliant, we also know that these occurrences match, i.e. they carry the same message.
Together, it follows that

w\in\{x\cdot y\mid x={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m\text{ and }y={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m\text{ for some }m\in\mathcal{V}\}^{*}\kern 5.0pt.

With this, it is obvious that
w
is forwarding.

Suppose that
u
is infinite.
In this case, the same reasoning almost applies.
The only difference is that it is possible that some send events are unmatched.
However, because
u
respects
\beta
, we know that there can be at most
\beta(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
such unmatched send events.
Thus, for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}
, there can be at most one unmatched send event.
This is precisely the difference between almost forwarding and forwarding.

\square

It will not be sufficient to only check that an implementation is (almost) forwarding.
What is important is that the channel participant can react to all possible messages properly (and forwards them).
We call this
amicable
.

Definition 20
(Amicable)

Let
A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
be an FSM over
\Sigma^{\mathsf{wCh}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
,

A_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}
be an FSM over
\Sigma^{\mathsf{wCh}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}
,
and
B
be the channel bound for
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle
.
We say that

A_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}

is
amicable
with

A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
if the following holds:

•

\mathcal{L}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
is channel-ordered,

•

A_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}
is forwarding, and

•

for every run of
A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
with trace
u
and

w=u{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}}=w_{1}\ldots
,
there is a run of
A_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}
with trace

w_{i}\cdot w^{\prime}_{0}\cdot w_{i+1\cdot B}\cdot w^{\prime}_{1}\cdot w_{i+2\cdot B}\cdot w^{\prime}_{2}\cdot\ldots

where,
for every
j
,

w^{\prime}_{j}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m

if

w_{i+j\cdot B}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m
,
and for every trace with prefix

w_{i}\cdot w^{\prime}_{0}\cdot\ldots w_{i+j\cdot B}
,
the only continuation is
w^{\prime}_{j}
.

We say a CSM
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
is amicable if,
for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
,

A_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}

is
amicable
with

A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}

for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{Ch}}
.

Given an amicable projection of an encoded PSM, there will be FSMs for channel participants.
We observe that we can match the states of such FSMs to different letters which ought to be forwarded.

Proposition 2
(Matching states and messages for amicable CSMs)

Let
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
be an amicable CSM that satisfies feasible eventual reception.
For every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{Ch}}
with

A_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}=(Q,\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}},\delta,q_{0},F)
,
there is a function
f\colon Q\to\mathcal{V}\uplus\{\varepsilon\}

such that:

•

if
f(q)=\varepsilon
, for every
(q,x,q^{\prime})\in\delta
, it holds that

x={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m
for some
m\in\mathcal{V}
,

•

if
f(q)=m
, then
q
is not final and for every
(q,x,q^{\prime})\in\delta
, it holds that

x={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
for some
m\in\mathcal{V}
.

We now prove that amicable CSMs with channel participants can be used to mimic CSMs without channel participants and vice versa, when the right encoding and decoding functions for words is applied.

Lemma 8

Let
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
be an amicable CSM. For every word
w\in(\Gamma^{\mathsf{wCh}})^{\infty}

that respects
\beta
,
it holds that

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}})

iff
\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w))\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})
.

Proof

We show that the run for
\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)
in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}

can be simulated in

\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}

for
\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w))
,
and vice versa.

Prior, we establish some notation that will be used for both cases.
We denote
\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)\coloneq\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{1})\cdot\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{2})\cdot\ldots
,
assuming that
w\coloneq w_{1}\cdot w_{2}\cdot\ldots

where
w_{i}\in\Gamma
for every
i
.
Let
\rho
be the run in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
for
\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)
:

(\vec{s}_{0},\vec{t}_{0},\xi^{\prime}_{0})\xrightarrow{\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{1})}\!\!^{2}\,(\vec{s}_{1},\vec{t}_{1},\xi^{\prime}_{1})\xrightarrow{\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{2})}\!\!^{2}\,\ldots

where we split the states for participants from
\mathcal{P}
, given by
\vec{s}_{i}
,
and the ones from
\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{Ch}}
, given by
\vec{t}_{i}
.
Let
\rho^{\prime}
be the run in

\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}

for
\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w))
:

(\vec{q}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{1}))}(\vec{q}_{1},\xi_{1})\xrightarrow{\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{2}))}\ldots
.

Compared to the proof of

Lemma

6
,
we are not given
\beta^{!}
and
\beta^{?}
explicitly.
However, we can construct them from the part of
w
which has been consumed already.
Formally, we define

\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t},w_{1}\ldots w_{l},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)\coloneq u_{i}\ldots u_{j}

for the states
\vec{t}
of channel participants and a prefix
w_{1}\ldots w_{l}
of
w
if the following holds:

•

j\equiv\lvert(w_{1}\ldots w_{i}){\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!\_}\rvert
,

•

i\equiv\lvert(w_{1}\ldots w_{l}){\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?\_}\rvert
,

•

for every
i\leq k<j
, it holds that
\vec{t}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{k}}}}}}=q^{\prime}
with
f(q^{\prime})=m
and
w_{k}=m
, and

•

for every
j\leq k<i
, it holds that
\vec{t}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{k}}}}}}=q^{\prime}
with
f(q^{\prime})=\varepsilon
.

where we use the function
f(\hbox{-})
from

Proposition

2

to distinguish between send and receive states of the channel participants.
Note that we use the same name
\operatorname{cont}(\hbox{-},\hbox{-},\hbox{-})
but different parameters.
Also, we have
\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t},\varepsilon,\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\varepsilon
by definition.
In fact, this only happens for initial states and this coincides with the fact that all channel participants are in their initial states initially, not storing any messages in transit.

First, let us assume there exists run
\rho

for
\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)

in
\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
.
We claim the following:
for every
i

with

(\vec{s}_{i},\vec{t}_{i},\xi^{\prime}_{i})\xrightarrow{\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})}\!\!^{k}\,(\vec{s}_{i+1},\vec{t}_{i+1},\xi^{\prime}_{i+1})

for
k\in\{1,2\}

and

(\vec{q}_{i},\xi_{i})

such that

(a)

\vec{q}_{i}=\vec{s}_{i}
,

(b)

\xi^{\prime}_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(c)

\xi^{\prime}_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\varepsilon

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(d)

\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},w_{1}\ldots w_{i-1},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

there is

(\vec{q}_{i+1},\xi_{i+1})

such that

(a’)

\vec{q}_{i+1}=\vec{s}_{i+1}
,

(b’)

\xi^{\prime}_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(c’)

\xi^{\prime}_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\varepsilon

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(d’)

\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i+1},w_{1}\ldots w_{i},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
, and

(e’)

(\vec{q}_{i},\xi_{i})\xrightarrow{w_{i}}(\vec{q}_{i+1},\xi_{i+1})
.

To use this claim, we need to argue that the initial states are related, i.e. the conditions hold for
i=0
.
Conditions

(a)

to

(c)

are trivially satisfied.
For
(d)
,
it suffices to recall that
\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t},\varepsilon,\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\varepsilon
.

Now, we prove the claim.
Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
We assume that

(a)

to

(d)

hold and prove

(a’)

to

(e’)
.
We use

(e’)

to obtain

(\vec{q}_{i+1},\xi_{i+1})
.
We will still need to show, though, that this transition is possible.
Let us do a case analysis on the shape of
w_{i}
.
We do a simultaneous case analysis whether
w_{i}
is a send or receive transition and whether the corresponding channel is in the domain of
\beta
.

•

w_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
and

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
:

Because of
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
, we have that

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})=w_{i}
.
Participant
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
is in the same state in
\vec{s}
and
\vec{q}
by

(a)
.
Since
w_{i}
is a send transition, it is always enabled and, thus, possible.
The channels change in the same way in both runs:
m
is appended to
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle
.
The states also change in the same way: only the one of
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
changes but in the same way due to determinism.
Thus, it is easy to check that all properties

(a’)

to

(d’)

are satisfied.

•

w_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
and

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
:

By definition,

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{\to}\kern 0.39993pt{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{:}\kern 0.39993ptm

for

\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{j}}}\equiv\lvert(w_{1}\ldots w_{i-1}){\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!\_}\rvert
.
Thus, there are two transitions in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
:
one send transition labelled with

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}!m

and one receive transition labelled with

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m
.
Participant
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
is in the same state in
\vec{s}
and
\vec{q}
by

(a)
.
Since
w_{i}
is a send transition, it is always enabled and, thus, possible.

(a’)
holds by determinism of the state machines for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.

(b’)
is satisfied as we deal with a transition for which

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
.

(c’)
is satisfied as the only message enqueued into a channel, with the first transition, is immediately received, with the second one.
For the remaining property, we show that the changes related to the second transition match the ones of the channel in
\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.
It is obvious that we only need to consider the changes of the channel
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle
.
For this, we have

\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)\cdot m
.
With

\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},w_{1}\ldots w_{i-1},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
,
it suffices to show that

\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},w_{1}\ldots w_{i-1},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)\cdot m=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i+1},w_{1}\ldots w_{i},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
.
And this easily follows from the definition of
\operatorname{cont}(\hbox{-},\hbox{-},\hbox{-})
because the only state to change is the one of
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}
and adding
w_{i}
changes the indices in the way that the state of this channel participant is considered.

•

w_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m
and

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
:

Because of
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
, we have that

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})=w_{i}
.
Participant
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
is in the same state in
\vec{s}
and
\vec{q}
by

(a)
.
From
(b)
and the fact that the transition is possible in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
, we know that the transition is also possible in
\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.
The channels change in the same way in both runs:
m
is removed from
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle
.
The states also change in the same way: only the one of
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
changes but in the same way due to determinism.
Thus, it is easy to check that all properties

(a’)

to

(d’)

are satisfied.

•

w_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m
and

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
:

By definition,

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{\to}\kern 0.39993pt{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{:}\kern 0.39993ptm

for

\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{j}}}\equiv\lvert(w_{1}\ldots w_{i-1}){\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?\_}\rvert
.
Thus, there are two transitions in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
:
one send transition labelled with

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m

and one receive transition labelled with

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}?m
.
In
\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
, we only have one transition.

We first argue that this transition is possible:
participant
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
is in the same state in
\vec{s}
and
\vec{q}
by

(a)
so it is able to receive
m
and,
from
(d)
, we know that
m
is enqueued at the head of
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle
in
\xi_{i}
.

It remains to show that the other properties are satisfied, for which the arguments are analogous.
We still repeat them for understandability.

(a’)
holds by determinism of the state machines for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.

(b’)
is satisfied as we deal with a transition for which

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
.

(c’)
is satisfied as the only message enqueued into a channel, with the first transition, is immediately received, with the second one.
For the remaining property, we show that the changes related to the first transition match the ones of the channel in
\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.
It is obvious that we only need to consider the changes of the channel
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle
.
For this, we have

\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=m\cdot\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
.
With

\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},w_{1}\ldots w_{i-1},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
,
it suffices to show that

\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},w_{1}\ldots w_{i-1},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=m\cdot\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i+1},w_{1}\ldots w_{i},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)
.
And this easily follows from the definition of
\operatorname{cont}(\hbox{-},\hbox{-},\hbox{-})
because the only state to change is the one of
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{j}}}}}
and adding
w_{i}
changes the indices in the way that the state of this channel participant is considered.

This concludes the proof for this direction.

Second, let us assume there exists run
\rho^{\prime}
for

\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
for
w
.
We claim the following:
for every
i

with

(\vec{q}_{i},\xi_{i})\xrightarrow{w_{i}}(\vec{q}_{i+1},\xi_{i+1})

and

(\vec{s}_{i},\vec{t}_{i},\xi^{\prime}_{i})

such that

(a)

\vec{q}_{i}=\vec{s}_{i}
,

(b)

\xi^{\prime}_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(c)

\xi^{\prime}_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\varepsilon

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(d)

\xi_{i}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i},w_{1}\ldots w_{i-1},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

there is

(\vec{s}_{i+1},\vec{t}_{i+1},\xi^{\prime}_{i+1})

such that

(a’)

\vec{q}_{i+1}=\vec{s}_{i+1}
,

(b’)

\xi^{\prime}_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\notin\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(c’)

\xi^{\prime}_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\varepsilon

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
,

(d’)

\xi_{i+1}(\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)=\operatorname{cont}(\vec{t}_{i+1},w_{1}\ldots w_{i},\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle)

for every
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\rangle\in\operatorname{dom}(\beta)
, and

(e’)

(\vec{s}_{i},\vec{t}_{i},\xi^{\prime}_{i})\xrightarrow{\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w_{i})}(\vec{s}_{i+1},\vec{t}_{i+1},\xi^{\prime}_{i+1})
.

The conditions are basically the same.
Thus, the initial states are still related, making the above claim sufficient to show our simulation argument.
The proof is analogous to the one before and, thus, omitted.
There is one minor difference: one exploits the fact that a CSM is amicable to show that the channel participants faithfully forward the respective messages.

Equipped with these, we can show that both ways of the encoding are fine to take.

Lemma 9

Let
M
be a PSM and
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
and
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
be CSMs.

(a)

If
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
is an amicable projection of
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
,

then
\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is a projection of
M
.

(b)

If
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is a projection of
M
, then
\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
is a projection of
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
.

Proof

First, we prove
(a)
.
Thus, we assume that

\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
is deadlock-free and forwarding as well as

\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}})=\mathcal{S}(\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M))
.
We prove deadlock freedom and protocol fidelity.

For deadlock freedom,
we assume, towards a contradiction,
