\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}

has a deadlock.
Let
w
be the trace of this run ending in a deadlock.
We used a simulation argument to show that there is a run with trace
\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)
in

\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}

in the second part of the proof for

Lemma

8
.
We can apply this result and reach also reach a configuration in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
from which no further transitions can be taken.
By determinism of each
A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
, this configuration is unique.
Hence, this is a deadlock and, yielding a contradiction.

For protocol fidelity, the following equivalences prove the claim:

w\in\mathcal{S}(M)

\overset{\ref{def:channel-encoding-prop-words-and-psm-func-align}}{\Leftrightarrow}

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)\in\mathcal{S}(\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M))

\overset{Assumption}{\Leftrightarrow}

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w)\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}})

\overset{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{lm:channel-encoding-prop-words-and-csm-func-rev}}{\Leftrightarrow}

\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w))\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})

\overset{\ref{def:channel-encoding-prop-words-almost-bij-1}}{\Leftrightarrow}

w\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})

Second, we prove
(b)
.
Thus, we assume that

\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is deadlock-free and

\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})=\mathcal{S}(M)
.
We prove deadlock freedom and protocol fidelity.

For deadlock freedom,
we assume, towards a contradiction,

\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}

has a deadlock.
Let
w
be the trace of this run ending in a deadlock.
By construction of channel participants, there is
w^{\prime}\in(\Sigma^{\mathsf{wCh}})^{*}
such that
w^{\prime}\sim w
.
Thus, there is
u\in\Gamma^{*}
such that
w^{\prime}=\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(u)
.
We used a simulation argument to show that there is a run with trace
u
in

\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}

in the second part of the proof for

Lemma

6
.
We can apply this result and also reach a configuration in
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
from which no further transitions can be taken.
By determinism of each
B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
, this configuration is unique.
Hence, this is a deadlock and, yielding a contradiction.

For protocol fidelity, the following equivalences prove the claim:

w\in\mathcal{S}(\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M))

\overset{\text{def.\ \& constr.}}{\Leftrightarrow}

\exists w^{\prime}\in(\Sigma^{\mathsf{wCh}})^{\infty}\text{ such that }w^{\prime}\sim w\text{ and }w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M))

\overset{\ref{def:channel-encoding-prop-words-and-psm-func-rev}}{\Leftrightarrow}

\exists w^{\prime}\in(\Sigma^{\mathsf{wCh}})^{\infty}\text{ such that }w^{\prime}\sim w\text{ and }\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w^{\prime})\in\mathcal{S}(M)

\overset{Assumption}{\Leftrightarrow}

\exists w^{\prime}\in(\Sigma^{\mathsf{wCh}})^{\infty}\text{ such that }w^{\prime}\sim w\text{ and }\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w^{\prime})\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})

\overset{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{def:channel-encoding-prop-words-and-csm-func-align}}{\Leftrightarrow}

\exists w^{\prime}\in(\Sigma^{\mathsf{wCh}})^{\infty}\text{ such that }w^{\prime}\sim w\text{ and }

\texttt{enc}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w^{\prime}))\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}})

\overset{\ref{def:channel-encoding-prop-words-almost-bij-2}}{\Leftrightarrow}

\exists w^{\prime}\in(\Sigma^{\mathsf{wCh}})^{\infty}\text{ such that }w^{\prime}\sim w\text{ and }w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}})

\overset{(*)}{\Leftrightarrow}

w\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}})

where the first equivalence follows by definition of the semantics but we keep the semantics
\mathcal{S}(\hbox{-})
instead of the language
\mathcal{L}(\hbox{-})
, and
(*)
follows from the fact that CSMs are closed under
\sim

[
60
, Lm. 22]
.
Furthermore,

\texttt{dec}_{\Gamma^{\infty}}(w^{\prime})
respects
\beta
because every word in
\mathcal{S}(M)
does
(
Proposition

1
)
and
w^{\prime}
is channel-ordered by

(3)
.
\square

It remains to show that we can obtain an amicable projection.
For this, we use the fact that we can obtain a local language preserving projection.

Definition 21
(Local language preserving projection
[
57
]
)

Let
L\subseteq\Gamma^{\infty}
be a language and
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
be a projection of
L
.
We say that
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is
local language preserving
if it holds that

L{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=\mathcal{L}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})

for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
.
For a CSM
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
to be local language preserving, we require

L{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=\mathcal{L}(B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})

for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}
.

Lemma 10
(
[
57
]
)

Let
M
be a projectable sink-final sender-driven
Σ1-PSM
and let
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
be its subset projection from
[
57
, Def. 5.4]
.
The subset projection can be computed in PSPACE and
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is a local language preserving projection.

Proof

Their subset projection is defined for global types but the algorithm works on a global state machine which is a sink-final
Σ1-PSM
.
The rest follows from
[
57
, Cor. 8.2]
.

\square

Lemma 11

Let
M
be a PSM and let
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
be a CSM.
If
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
is a local language preserving projection for
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
, then
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
is amicable.

Proof

For every channel participant
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}
, we have to show that
A_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}
is amicable with
A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}
be a channel participant.
By construction of
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}
, we know that

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}
solely receives from
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
in
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
.
From

Lemma

7
,
we know that

\mathcal{S}(\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)){\Downarrow}_{\Gamma^{\mathsf{wCh}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}}

is forwarding or almost forwarding (only possible if the projected word is infinite).
Since
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
is a local language preserving projection of
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
,
we know that all words in

\mathcal{S}(\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)){\Downarrow}_{\Gamma^{\mathsf{wCh}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}}

have a trace in
A_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}
.
Last, by construction,
\mathcal{S}(\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)){\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
is channel-ordered.
Thus, again, by local language preservation,
A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
is channel-ordered.
Thus, it follows that
A_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})_{i}}}}}}
is amicable with
A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.

\square

Our main theorem combines the previous observations.

See
4.1

Proof

Let
(M,\beta)
be a Tame PSM, i.e. be sender-driven, sink-final and respect channel bounds
\beta
.
We apply the channel-participant encoding to obtain
the sink-final
Σ1-PSM

\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
.

We claim that
M
is projectable if and only if
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
is projectable.

For the direction from left to right,
let
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
be the witness for projectability of
M
.
With
Lemma

9
(b)
,

\{\!\!\{\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
is a projection of
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
, proving its projectability.

For the direction from right to left,
we apply

Lemma

10

to obtain a local language preserving projection
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
for
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
.
With

Lemma

11
,
it follows that
\{\!\!\{A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is amicable.
By

Lemma

9
(a)
,

\{\!\!\{\texttt{dec}_{\operatorname{FSM}}(A_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\negmedspace\mathsf{wCh}}}
is a projection of
M
, proving its projectability.

This proves that
M
is projectable if and only if
\texttt{enc}_{\operatorname{PSM}}(M)
is projectable.

The encodings (and decodings) can be constructed in polynomial time.
Thus, we can check projectability and also obtain an projection of
M
in
PSPACE
(
Lemma

10
).

\square

0.B.3
Additional Material for
Section

4.2

See
4.2

Proof

We consider the problem of checking if a word is accepted by a Turing Machine.
This is known to be undecidable and we reduce it to checking projectability of a mixed-choice sink-final
Σ1-PSM
.
We basically construct a PSM with two branches at the top.
For each, we construct a language and they coincide – which will be necessary for projectability – if and only if the Turing Machine does not halt in a final configuration.
We assume familiarity with the concept of Turing Machines and refer to
[
45
]
for further details.

Let
\mathit{TM}
be a Turing Machine with tape alphabet
\Delta
and states
Q
such that
\Delta\cap Q=\emptyset
.
We have that
q_{0}\in Q
is the initial state and
q_{f}\in Q
is, without loss of generality, the only final state.
A configuration of
\mathit{TM}
is given by a word
a_{1},\ldots,a_{i},q,b_{1},\ldots,b_{j}\in\Delta^{\!*}Q\Delta^{\!*}
. The initial configuration for input word
w
is

q_{0}w
while any configuration from
\Delta^{\!*}q_{f}\Delta^{\!*}
is final.
A computation is a sequence of configurations

(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})
such that

u_{i+1}
is the next configuration of
\mathit{TM}
,
also denoted by
u_{i}\vdash_{\mathit{TM}}u_{i+1}
.
A computation
(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})
accepts
w
if
u_{1}=q_{0}w
and
u_{m}\in\Delta^{\!*}q_{f}\Delta^{\!*}
.

For our encoding, we use five participants
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}
who send configurations to each other.
Thus, messages are from the set

\Delta\uplus\{\circ,{\langle\negthinspace\langle},{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle},\bot\}\uplus Q

where
\circ
is sent by
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}
to indicate the start of a new pair of configurations
and
{\langle\negthinspace\langle}
and
{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}
delimit a configuration.

We introduce the notation

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m

to abbreviate

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{\to}\kern 0.39993pt{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{:}\kern 0.39993ptm\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{\to}\kern 0.39993pt{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\kern 0.39993pt{:}\kern 0.39993ptm
.
We only specify interactions using

\_\leftrightarrow\_:\_
.
Using these, we will also define regular expressions and complements thereof and consider

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m

as their single letters.
By construction, every PSM will be Σ
1
-bounded and, in fact, every message is immediately acknowledged.

For a word
w=w_{1}\ldots w_{i}
, we write

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:w

for

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:w_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:w_{i}
.

For words
C_{1},D_{1},C_{2},D_{2}\ldots,C_{m},D_{m}\in(\Delta\uplus Q)^{*}
, we define the word

\displaystyle w(C_{1},D_{1},C_{2},D_{2},\ldots,C_{m},D_{m})\coloneq\hskip 8.19447pt

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:C_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:D_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:C_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:D_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:C_{m}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:D_{m}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\phantom{\cdot}\kern 4.09723pt.

Intuitively,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}
sends the sequence
C_{i}
to
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}
while

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}
sends the sequence
D_{i}
to
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}
.
Each sequence is started by a
{\langle\negthinspace\langle}
-message and finished by a
{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}
-message between the respective pair.
The participant
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}
starts each round by sending
\circ
.

Note that the communication of
C_{i}
between
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}
can happen concurrently to both
D_{i-1}
and
D_{i}
between
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}
.
(This will later allow us to both detect if
C_{i}
and
D_{i}
do not coincide or
C_{i}
is no successor configuration of
D_{i}
.)

We define two languages
L_{l}
and
L_{r}
, which we later use for two branches of the PSM encoding.

\displaystyle L_{l}

\displaystyle\coloneq\{\mathcal{C}(w(C_{1},D_{1},\ldots,C_{m},D_{m}))\mid m\geq 1,C_{1},D_{1},\ldots,C_{m},D_{m}\in(\Delta\uplus Q)^{*}\}

\displaystyle L_{r}

\displaystyle\coloneq L_{l}\setminus\{\mathcal{C}(w(u_{1},u_{1},\ldots,u_{m},u_{m}))\mid(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})\text{ is an accepting computation}\}

To make the resulting PSM sink-final, we define a sequence of messages that indicates the end of an execution:

w_{\mathit{end}}\coloneq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\bot\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:\bot\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\bot\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:\bot

and append it to obtain

L^{\prime}_{l}\coloneq\{w\cdot w_{\mathit{end}}\mid w\in L_{l}\}

and

L^{\prime}_{r}\coloneq\{w\cdot w_{\mathit{end}}\mid w\in L_{r}\}
.

We will show that both
L_{l}
and
L_{r}
, and thus,
L^{\prime}_{l}
and
L^{\prime}_{r}
, can be specified as Σ1-PSMs.
Provided with PSMs for
L_{l}
and
L_{r}
, it is straightforward to construct a
M_{\mathit{TM}}
such that

\mathcal{S}(M_{\mathit{TM}})=\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\kern 0.39993pt{\to}\kern 0.39993pt{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\kern 0.39993pt{:}\kern 0.39993pt\mathsf{l}\cdot w\mid w\in L^{\prime}_{l}\}\uplus\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\kern 0.39993pt{\to}\kern 0.39993pt{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\kern 0.39993pt{:}\kern 0.39993pt\mathsf{r}\cdot w\mid w\in L^{\prime}_{r}\}\kern 5.0pt.

By definition of
L^{\prime}_{l}
and
L^{\prime}_{r}
, every word ends with
w_{\mathit{end}}
so
M_{\mathit{TM}}
is sink-final.
(In fact, if there is an implementation, the FSM for each participant will also be sink-final.)
We will show that
M_{\mathit{TM}}
is projectable if and only if
\mathit{TM}
does not accept the input
w
.

For this, it suffices to establish the following four facts:

•

Claim 1:
L_{l}
and
L_{r}
can be specified as Σ1-PSMs.

•

Claim 2:
L_{l}
is projectable.

•

Claim 3: If
\mathit{TM}
has no accepting computation for
w
, then
\mathcal{L}(M_{\mathit{TM}})
is projectable.

•

Claim 4: If
\mathit{TM}
has an accepting computation for
w
, then
\mathcal{L}(M_{\mathit{TM}})
is not projectable.

Claim 1:

Both
L_{l}
and
L_{r}
can be specified as Σ1-PSMs.

Proof of Claim 1.

It is easy to construct a PSM from a regular expression.
Thus, for conciseness, we give regular expressions for the languages we consider or for their complements.
For this, we introduce some more notation for concise specifications when using sets of messages:

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}\coloneq({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:x_{1}+\ldots+{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:x_{n})\kern 5.0pt.

First, let us consider
L_{l}
.
Inspired by the definition of
w(C_{1},D_{1},\ldots,C_{m},D_{m})
, we construct this regular expression
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl}
for
L_{l}
:

\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:(\Delta\uplus Q))^{*}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle\phantom{(}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:(\Delta\uplus Q))^{*}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle})^{*}\kern 5.0pt.

Second, let us consider
L_{r}
.
Recall that
L_{r}
should admit the encoding of all sequences of configurations except for accepting ones.
We provide an exhaustive list of how such a sequence can fail to be an accepting computation.
We provide a language
L_{r,i}
for each and
L_{r}
is their union.
1
1

1

We renumbered the languages because some of Lohrey’s construction does not apply to this undecidability proof.

•

L_{r,1}
contains all sequences of configurations for which some
C_{k}
or
D_{k}
is actually not a configuration, i.e. not from
\Delta^{\!*}Q\Delta^{\!*}
.

•

L_{r,2}
contains all sequences for which
C_{1}
is not the correct initial configuration, i.e. it does not have the shape
q_{0},a_{1},a_{2},\ldots a_{n}
where
w=a_{1}\cdots a_{n}
.

•

L_{r,3}
contains all sequences for which
q_{f}
does not occur in
C_{m}
.

•

L_{r,4}
contains all sequences where
C_{k}
and
D_{k}
differ in some position.

•

L_{r,5}
contains all sequences for which
C_{k+1}
is no successor configuration for
D_{k}
.

For each
L_{r,i}
, we show that it can be specified as PSM (or regular expression).
It is straightforward to obtain a PSM for
L_{r}
by adding one initial state and adding a transition from this one to the initial state for the PSM of
L_{r,i}
for each
i
.

Language
L_{r,1}
:

We construct a regular expression for

w(C_{1},D_{1},\ldots,C_{m},D_{m})
for any
m
such that there is some
C_{i}
or
D_{i}
with either no message from
Q
or at least two messages from
Q
:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:\Delta\uplus Q)^{*}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot\phantom{s}

Let us explain how
{r}_{\kern-0.40974pt1}
works.
In the beginning, there are only
C_{i}
and
D_{i}
with one message from
Q
.
At some point, one of the regular expressions

{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl0},{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl2},{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptr0}
or
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptr2}

has to match.
These specify some way how the number of messages for
Q
can be wrong:

{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl0}
has no messages from
Q
between
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}
while

{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptr0}
has no messages from
Q
between
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}
;

{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl2}
has more than one message from
Q
between
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}
while

{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptr2}
has more than one message from
Q
between
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}
.
For each, the other pair can communicate any number of messages from
Q
to account for sequences where both
C_{i}
and
D_{i}
do not match.
Subsequently, we use
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl}
to simply allow a sequence of any configuration.

Language
L_{r,2}
:

Let
w=a_{1}\ldots a_{n}
be the input word for
\mathit{TM}
.
Then, we can specify
L_{r,2}
as follows:

\{\mathcal{C}(w(C_{1},D_{1},C_{2},D_{2}\ldots,C_{m},D_{m}))\mid m\geq 1\land C_{1}\neq q_{0},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\}

It is easy to see that we can change
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl}
to obtain a regular expression for

\{w(C_{2},D_{2},\ldots,C_{m},D_{m})\mid m\geq 1\}
.
Thus, it suffices to show that

w(C_{1},D_{1})

with
C_{1}\neq q_{0},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}

can be specified as PSM.
Again, we give a regular expression for the complement.
We observe that the communication between
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}
about the configuration can easily be specified as regular expression:

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:q_{0}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:a_{1}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:a_{n}\kern 5.0pt.

It is straightforward to construct a PSM for the complement of this regular expression.
(Before we did not use the complement for
L_{r,1}
because we could not guarantee that the same number of configurations would be communicated between both pairs.)
When combined, this gives us the following regular expression (with the complement operator as syntactic sugar) for
L_{r,2}
:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:q_{0}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:a_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:a_{n}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:(\Delta\uplus Q))^{*}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl}\kern 5.0pt.

Language
L_{r,3}
:

The following regular expression specifies all sequences for which the last
C_{m}
does not contain the final state
q_{f}\in Q
:

\displaystyle{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:(\Delta\uplus Q\setminus\{q_{f}\}))^{*}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot\phantom{s}

Language
L_{r,4}
:

Intuitively, we can merge the loops for both
C_{i}
and
D_{i}
to check that some message at the same position is different.
This is possible because all languages are closed under
\sim
by definition.
We introduce this notation

\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:x\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:x\mid x\in\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}\}\}

which is an abbreviation for

({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:y_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:y_{1})+\ldots+({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:y_{n}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:y_{n})\kern 5.0pt.

With this, the following is a regular expression for
L_{r,4}
:

\displaystyle{r}_{\kern-0.24501pt4}\coloneq\hskip 8.19447pt

\displaystyle{r}_{\kern-0.24501ptl}\cdot

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle(\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:x_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:x_{1}\mid x_{1}\in(\Delta\cup Q)\})^{*}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle({r}_{\kern-0.24501pta}+{r}_{\kern-0.24501ptb}+{r}_{\kern-0.24501ptc})\cdot{r}_{\kern-0.24501ptl}

where

\displaystyle{r}_{\kern-0.24501pta}\coloneq\hskip 8.19447pt

\displaystyle(\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:x_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:x_{3}\mid x_{2},x_{3}\in(\Delta\cup Q)\text{ and }x_{2}\neq x_{3}\})\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt(\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:x_{4}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:x_{5}\mid x_{4},x_{5}\in(\Delta\cup Q)\})^{*}\cdot{r}_{\kern-0.24501ptl}

\displaystyle{r}_{\kern-0.24501ptb}\coloneq\hskip 8.19447pt

\displaystyle\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:x_{7}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\mid x_{7}\in(\Delta\cup Q)\}\cdot({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:\Delta\cup Q)^{*}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot{r}_{\kern-0.24501ptl}

\displaystyle{r}_{\kern-0.24501ptc}\coloneq\hskip 8.19447pt

\displaystyle\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:x_{6}\mid x_{6}\in(\Delta\cup Q)\}\cdot({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:\Delta\cup Q)^{*}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot{r}_{\kern-0.24501ptl}\kern 4.09723pt.

The regular expression checks that at some point two configurations
C_{i}
and
D_{i}
do not agree on some position (using
{r}_{\kern-0.40974pta}
), or
C_{i}
is longer than
D_{i}
(using
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptb}
), or
D_{i}
is longer than
C_{i}
(using
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptc}
).

Language
L_{r,5}
:

We use the same idea of merging the loops to compare as for the previous case and also use the same notation.
We can give a regular expression that consists of different phases.
First, we let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}
communicate about
C_{1}
in order to then compare
D_{i}
with
C_{i+1}
for any
i
in a loop.
We want that
C_{i+1}
is no successor of
D_{i}
for some
i
.
Thus, we check if the changes from
D_{i}
to
C_{i+1}
are a valid transition for
\mathit{TM}
.
The regular expression
{r}_{\kern-0.40974pt5}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle{r}_{\kern-0.175pt5}\coloneq\hskip 7.3612pt

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:(\Delta\uplus Q))^{*}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle({r}_{\kern-0.175ptd}+{r}_{\kern-0.175pte})^{*}\cdot({r}_{\kern-0.175ptf}+{r}_{\kern-0.175ptg})\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:(\Delta\uplus Q))^{*}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\mid a_{1},a_{2},b_{1},b_{2},c_{2}\in(\Delta\cup Q)^{*},a_{1}\neq a_{2}\text{ and }\exists w_{1}\in\Delta^{\!*}.\,w_{1}a_{1}b_{1}\vdash_{\mathit{TM}}w_{1}a_{2}b_{2}c_{2}\})

\displaystyle{r}_{\kern-0.175ptf}\coloneq\hskip 7.3612pt

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle(\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:x_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:x_{1}\mid x_{1}\in(\Delta\cup Q)\})^{*}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle(\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:a_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:a_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:b_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:b_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:c_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:c_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\mid a_{1},a_{2},b_{1},b_{2},c_{1},c_{2}\in(\Delta\cup Q)^{*},a_{1}\neq a_{2}\text{ and }\nexists w_{1},w_{2}\in\Delta^{\!*}.\,w_{1}a_{1}b_{1}c_{1}w_{2}\vdash_{\mathit{TM}}w_{1}a_{2}b_{2}c_{2}w_{2}\})\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:\Delta\cup Q)^{*}\cdot({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:\Delta\cup Q)^{*}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}

\displaystyle{r}_{\kern-0.175ptg}\coloneq\hskip 7.3612pt

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle(\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:x_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:x_{1}\mid x_{1}\in(\Delta\cup Q))^{*}\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle(\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:c_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}\mid c_{1}\in(\Delta\cup Q)^{*}\})\cdot\phantom{s}

\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:\Delta\cup Q)^{*}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{5}:{\rangle\negthinspace\rangle}

We distinguish two types of transitions:
the ones that simply change letters in the middle of the configurations (
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptd}
) and the ones that extend the tape (
{r}_{\kern-0.40974pte}
).
If one is matched against, we recurse using

({r}_{\kern-0.40974ptd}+{r}_{\kern-0.40974pte})^{*}
.
If not,
({r}_{\kern-0.40974ptf}+{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptg})
is matched against and we, subsequently, allow any possible subsequent pair configurations using
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl}
.
The regular expression
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptf}
checks that the transition is not possible while
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptg}
checks if
C_{i+1}
is shorter than
D_{i}
.
Without loss of generality, we can assume that the tape never shrinks (as it could be encoded using an extra tape alphabet letter).
Note that the transition check is a local condition and it suffices to check at most two more messages after the first different message.
In fact, the words
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
in the conditions do not matter:
either it is a transition for all such pairs or none.
After the mismatch, we let
D_{i}
catch up and continue with
{r}_{\kern-0.40974ptl}
.

Mixed choice
:

We explained how to construct a PSM for
L_{r,i}
for every
i
.
They are
Σ1-PSMs
by construction.
In fact, each of them individually also satisfies sender-driven choice.
However, when we combine both PSMs for
L_{r,4}
and
L_{r,5}
in order to obtain a PSM for
L_{r}
, the resulting PSM exposes mixed choice.
Intuitively, this happens because
L_{r,4}
checks
C_{i}
against
D_{i}
and
L_{r,5}
checks
D_{i}
against
C_{i+1}
.
Technically, when merging both PSMs, we reach a state after the sequence

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}:\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:{\langle\negthinspace\langle}

for which
L_{r,4}
requires to have

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}:\circ

next while

L_{r,5}
requires to have a loop with

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}\leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{1}:\Delta\uplus Q.

It is not possible to let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}
send a message to distinguish both branches as the indistinguishability of both branches is necessary so that
C_{i+1}
can be compared to both
D_{i}
and
D_{i+1}
.

End Proof of Claim 1.

Claim 2:

L_{l}
is projectable.

Proof of Claim 2.

By
Theorem

6.1
, the PSM for
L_{l}
can be represented as a global type with mixed choice.
It is also
0
-reachable
[
70
]
, i.e. one can reach a final state from every state.
For a
0
-reachable global type
G
,

[
70
, Lm. 4.10]
showed that projections for
\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{fin}}(G)
generalise to
\mathcal{L}_{\inf}(G)
.
They do not consider mixed choice but the proof generalises to the mixed choice setting as it does not use any restrictions on choice.

[
2
]
showed that a language
L
of finite words is projectable if and only if two closure conditions
CC_{2}
and
CC_{3}
hold.
Hence, it suffices to show that both
CC_{2}
and
CC_{3}
.

In what follows, a word
u
is said to be complete if

u{\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!\_}=u{\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?\_}
.

CC_{2}
:
If
w\in\Gamma^{*}
is
FIFO-compliant, complete,
and for every participant
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
, there is
v\in L
with

w{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=v{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
,
then
w\in L
.

CC_{3}
:
If
w
is FIFO-compliant and for every participant
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
, there is
v\in\operatorname{pref}(L)
with

w{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=v{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
,
then
v\in\operatorname{pref}(L)
.

We show that
L_{l}
satisfies
CC_{2}
.
The proof for
CC_{3}
is analogous.
Let
w\in\Gamma^{*}
be a FIFO-compliant and complete word such that for every participant
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
,
there is
v\in L_{l}
with

w{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=v{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
Let us give the structure of
w{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{i}}}
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
.

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}}}=\hskip 8.50012pt

\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}!\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{2}?\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}!\circ\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{3}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{4}?\circ)^{k_{3}}\text{ for some }k_{3}
