:

\frac{\emptyset\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}s_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]::\varepsilon,s_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]::\varepsilon\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s_{2}\blacktriangleright\varepsilon}{(6):\hskip 9.24994pts_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q_{0},\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}s_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]::l_{1}(q_{0}),s_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]::\varepsilon\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s_{2}\blacktriangleright\varepsilon[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}})\mapsto l_{1}\langle s_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]\rangle]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-Queue} }

The presentation of our type system is inspired by work from

Scalas and Yoshida [69]
.
Thus, we want to highlight key differences.
First, we handle sender-driven choice, which allows a participant to send to different receivers and to receive from different senders, while they only consider directed choice.
In fact, our processes can choose between different options when sending messages while their work restricts to a single choice.
However, their treatment could be combined with control flow like if-then-else to cover more scenarios.
Also,
[
69
]
employs subtyping similar to what we do for send actions so there can still be multiple branches when sending.
In a sender-driven choice setting, there are subtleties for subtyping as one cannot simply add receives for instance.
For details, we refer to
[
56
]
where CSMs are considered for subtyping and protocol refinement.
With our framework, one can directly make use of their results: we can apply their subtyping algorithm to obtain another CSM against which we type check.
Second,
[
69
]
only considers one error scenario:
a participant would like to receive something but the first message in the respective queue does not match.
We generalise this scenario to our setting and require that the first message in all respective queues does not match.
In addition, we consider the error case where a session ended with non-empty queues.

[
69
]
also considers S-deadlock freedom and we refer to a discussion in
Section

5
.
In the next section, we will prove that our type system prevents both these scenarios.

Remark 4
(Adding base types)

The treatment of base types and expressions in type systems is well-understood.
Hence, it should be straightforward to extend our type system to add expressions.
More specifically, one could then send the result of expressions and, hence, variables can be bound to values.
Provided with (Boolean) expressions, it is also standard to add features of control-flow like if-then-else.
For most flexible use of our results, it would make most sense if a user provided a type system for the expressions and base types they need.
Then, the type system would use what we defined for local types (and hence delegation) and the provided type system for expressions.
There is one important difference between both type systems.
While ours is linear, the one for expressions does not need to be.
With a non-linear type system, one can duplicate and drop type bindings from the typing context, using rules called
contraction
and
weakening
.

0.C.4
Soundness of Type System

For conciseness, we assume a process definition typing context
\Theta
, typing contexts

\Lambda
,
\Lambda_{1}
,
\Lambda_{2}
,
\ldots
,
and queue typing contexts

\Omega
,
\Omega_{1}
,
\Omega_{2}
,
\ldots

in this section.

We presented a type system for processes and runtime configurations.
While we closed the reduction semantics under structural precongruence
\sqsubseteq
, we have not stated the respective rules for our type system:

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P\hskip 9.24994ptP\sqsubseteq P^{\prime}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P^{\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={PT-$\sqsubseteq$} }\par\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R\hskip 9.24994ptR\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R^{\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\sqsubseteq$} }

We show that these rules are admissible, i.e. they can be added without changing the capabilities of the type system.
This allows us not to consider these rules in the following proofs but still use them if convenient.

Lemma 13
(Admissibility of structural precongruence for runtime configuration typing)

Let
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
be well-annotated runtime configurations.
If

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}

and

R_{1}\sqsubseteq R_{2}
,
then

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}
.

Proof

We first consider the cases for structural congruence
\equiv
and then the additional ones for structural precongruence.
We do a case analysis on
\equiv
and reason for both directions.
Subsequently, we consider the two rules for
\sqsubseteq
.

•

R_{1}\parallel R_{2}\equiv R_{2}\parallel R_{1}
:

By inversion, we know that
RT-
\parallel
is the first rule applied in the typing derivation.
This rule is symmetric so basically the typing derivation works.

•

(R_{1}\parallel R_{2})\parallel R_{3}\equiv R_{1}\parallel(R_{2}\parallel R_{3})
:

By inversion, we know that
RT-
\parallel
is the first and second rule applied in the typing derivation.
It is easy to see that the typing derivation can be rearranged to match the structure.

•

R\parallel\mathbf{0}\equiv R
:

First, assume that there is a typing derivation

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R\parallel\mathbf{0}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

We show there is a typing derivation

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R
.
Inversion yields that two rules can be applied for the given typing derivation

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}
:

RT-end
and

RT-
\mathbf{0}
.
Thus, it follows that

\Omega_{2}=\emptyset
.
Also,

\Lambda_{2}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in S}

for some set of participants
S

and

\operatorname{end}(\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})

for every

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in S
.
By inversion, there is a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R
.
With

\Omega_{2}=\emptyset
,
it remains to show that there is a typing derivation

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R
.
The only difference is the typing context

\Lambda_{2}
.
This, however, can be taken care of using
RT-end
as in the other typing derivation, concluding this case.

Second, assume there is a typing derivation for
R
.
We show there is a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R\parallel\mathbf{0}
.
We first apply
RT-
\parallel
to obtain

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\mathbf{0}$} }}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R\parallel\mathbf{0}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

for which the right premise is met with
RT-
\mathbf{0}
and the left premise is given by assumption.

•

(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,(\upnu s^{\prime}:\mathcal{B})\,R\equiv(\upnu s^{\prime}:\mathcal{B})\,(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R
:

By inversion, both typing derivations need to apply

RT-
\upnu
twice in the beginning.
It is straightforward that both rule applications do not interfere with each other, yielding the same premise to prove:

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s},\Lambda_{s^{\prime}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega_{s},\Omega_{s^{\prime}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R

Thus, this is given by assumption.

•

(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,(R_{1}\parallel R_{2})\equiv R_{1}\parallel(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R_{2}

and
s
is not free in
R_{1}
:

First, we assume there is a typing derivation for

(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,(R_{1}\parallel R_{2})

and show there is a typing derivation for

R_{1}\parallel(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R_{2}
.
Applying inversion twice yields

\frac{\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}\hskip 8.24454pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2},\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2},\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2},\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2},\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}\parallel R_{2}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{left={RT-$\parallel$} }(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})\hskip 8.50012pt\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 8.50012pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,(R_{1}\parallel R_{2})}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{right={RT-$\upnu$} }

where

\Lambda=\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}

and

\Omega=\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}
.
We claim we can assume that
\Lambda_{s}
and
\Omega_{s}
are used in the typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2},\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2},\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}
.
By definition, these only contain type bindings related to
s
, which does not occur in
R_{1}
by assumption.
There might exist a typing derivation where parts of
\Lambda_{s}
or
\Omega_{s}
appear in the typing derivation for
R_{1}
but these can only removed with the rules

RT-end
(not even with

RT-EmptyQueue
since this requires

s\blacktriangleright\varepsilon
).
Hence, such derivations can be mimicked in the typing derivation for
R_{2}
, justifying our treatment of
\Lambda_{s}
and
\Omega_{s}
.
We construct a typing derivation:

\frac{\frac{(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})\hskip 8.04996pt\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2},\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2},\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R_{2}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\upnu$} }\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}\parallel(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R_{2}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

All premises coincide with the ones of the original typing derivation, concluding this case.

Second, we assume there is a typing derivation for

R_{1}\parallel(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R_{2}

and show there is a typing derivation for

(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,(R_{1}\parallel R_{2})
.
The proof is analogous to the previous case but we do not need to reason about the treatment of
\Lambda_{s}
and
\Omega_{s}
but it suffices to show there is one typing and we can choose the respective treatment.

•

(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,s\blacktriangleright\varepsilon\sqsubseteq\mathbf{0}
:

We assume there is a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,s\blacktriangleright\varepsilon
.
We show there is a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}
.
By inversion, we know that
RT-
\upnu
is the last rule to be applied and we get one of the premises:

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\varepsilon

with

\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}
and
\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}
.
By inversion,

RT-EmptyQueue
and

RT-end
are the only rules that can be applied in the typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}
.
Thus, we have

\Omega=\emptyset
,
changing our proof obligation to

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}
.
Since

RT-EmptyQueue
does only change the queue typing context,
we have that

\Lambda=\bigcup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\{s^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{s^{\prime}}}

for a set of sessions
\mathcal{S}
that does not contain
s
and

\operatorname{end}(\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})

for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{s^{\prime}}
and
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
.
Therefore, we can also first apply
RT-end

\lvert\Lambda\rvert
times and
last
RT-EmptyQueue
to obtain a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}
.

This concludes the proof.

\square

We proved the admissibility lemma for the type system for runtime configurations.
The proof for the type system for processes is analogous for most cases.

Lemma 14
(Admissibility of structural precongruence for process typing)

Let
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
be well-annotated processes.
If it holds that

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{1}

and

P_{1}\sqsubseteq P_{2}
,
then

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{2}
.

Proof

The respective cases are analogous to the ones in the proof of

Lemma

13
.
We only need to consider the case where

(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,\mathbf{0}\sqsubseteq\mathbf{0}
:

We assume there is a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,\mathbf{0}
.
We show there is a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}
.
By inversion, we know that
PT-
\upnu
is the last rule to be applied and we get one of the premises:

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}

with

\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\operatorname{init}(\mathcal{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}
.
By inversion,

PT-
\mathbf{0}
and

PT-end
are the only rules that can be applied in the typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}
.
Since

PT-
\mathbf{0}
needs the second typing context to be empty, it is applied last and all other derivations are applications of

PT-end
.
Therefore,

\Lambda=\bigcup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\{s^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{s^{\prime}}}

for some set of sessions
\mathcal{S}
that does not contain
s

and

\operatorname{end}(\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})

for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{s^{\prime}}
and
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
.
Therefore, we can also first apply
RT-end

\lvert\Lambda\rvert
times and
last
RT-
\mathbf{0}
to obtain a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathbf{0}
.

\square

We proceed with a few observations about our type system that we will use later in the proof for our main result.

To start, we show that a term
x
cannot appear in a runtime configuration if there is no type binding for it in the typing context.

Lemma 15

Let
R
be a well-annotated runtime configuration.
If

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R

and
x
is not in
\Lambda
,
then
x
cannot occur in
R
.

Proof

Towards a contradiction, assume that
x
occurs in
R
.
Then, at some point in the typing derivation

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R

one of the following rules applies to handle
x
:

RT-
\mathsf{Q}
,

RT-
\IntCh
, or

RT-
\ExtCh
.
Each of them requires all variables to occur in their respective typing contexts, yielding a contradiction.

\square

We defined a type system for processes and one for runtime configurations.
Both are very similar and we defined runtime configurations to only have queues for active sessions.
Once a process becomes active, we turn it into a runtime configuration using
\lceil\hbox{-}\rceil
.
We show that this preserves typability with an empty queue typing context.

Lemma 16

Let
P
be a well-annotated process.
If

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P
,
then

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\lceil P\rceil
.

Proof

We prove this by induction on the structure of
P
.

For all except
P=P_{1}\parallel P_{2}
and
P=(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,P^{\prime}
, it holds that

\lceil P\rceil=P
.
For all typing rules that processes and runtime configurations share, the queue typing context is not changed in the respective runtime configuration typing rule.
Thus,

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\lceil P\rceil
.

For
P=P_{1}\parallel P_{2}
, the claim follows directly by induction hypothesis.

Last, we consider
P=(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,P^{\prime}
.
We have the following typing derivation

\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\operatorname{init}(\mathcal{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P^{\prime}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,P^{\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={PT-$\upnu$} }

We show there is a typing derivation where the last rule is
RT-
\upnu
.
This requires a reachable configuration in the respective CSM:

(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})
.
In this case, we choose the initial states and empty channels, which allows us to use
RT-EmptyQueue
for the queues.

\frac{\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P^{\prime}\hskip 8.04996pt\frac{}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\varepsilon}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{right={RT-EmptyQueue} }}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(P^{\prime}\parallel s\blacktriangleright\varepsilon)}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }\hskip 9.24994pt(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})\hskip 9.24994pt\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,(P^{\prime}\parallel s\blacktriangleright\varepsilon)}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\upnu$} }

where
\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}=\operatorname{init}(\mathcal{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})
for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
and
\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})=\varepsilon
for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
.
Thus,
(\vec{q},\xi)
is clearly reachable.
Also, both second typing contexts then coincide:
\Lambda^{\prime}=\Lambda_{s}
.
Thus,
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P^{\prime}
, the last premise to satisfy,
follows from the induction hypothesis.

\square

With the following lemma, we show that, if there is a typing derivation for a process, then the queue typing context is empty.

Lemma 17

If
P
is a well-annotated process such that

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P
,
then
\Omega=\emptyset
.

Proof

We do induction on the depth of the typing derivation.

For the base case, we consider
RT-
\mathbf{0}
, for which the claim trivially holds, and
RT-EmptyQueue
, for which we reach a contradiction because
s\blacktriangleright\sigma
is no process.

Let us turn to the induction step:

•

RT-
\mathsf{Q}
: trivially holds

•

RT-end
: by inversion and induction hypothesis

•

RT-
\IntCh
: by inversion and induction hypothesis for every
i\in I

•

RT-
\ExtCh
: by inversion and induction hypothesis for every
i\in I

•

RT-
\parallel
: by inversion and induction hypothesis twice

•

RT-
\upnu
: by inversion and induction hypothesis

•

RT-Queue
: contradiction because
s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto l\langle v\rangle\cdot\vec{m}]
is no process

This concludes the proof.

\square

In our type system, we type a queue from the first to the last element, when using
RT-Queue
.
Thus, when applying inversion for this rule, we only get the type for the first element of a non-empty queue.
The following lemma allows us to also obtain the type for its last element.

Lemma 18
(Message list reversal)

Let

\gamma

be a queue type.
If

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto\vec{m}],\text{ then}

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,v:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\gamma\cdot l(L)\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto\vec{m}\cdot l\langle v\rangle]\kern 5.0pt.

Proof

We prove this claim by induction on the length
n
of
\vec{m}=l_{1}\langle v_{1}\rangle\cdot\ldots\cdot l_{n}\langle v_{n}\rangle
.

If
n=0
, the claim is exactly the assumption.

For the induction step, we assume that

\vec{m}=l_{1}\langle v_{1}\rangle\cdot l_{2}\langle v_{2}\rangle\cdot\ldots\cdot l_{n}\langle v_{n}\rangle

and the induction hypothesis holds for
l_{2}\langle v_{2}\rangle\cdot\ldots\cdot l_{n}\langle v_{n}\rangle
.

We want to show that

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto l_{1}\langle v_{1}\rangle\cdot l_{2}\langle v_{2}\rangle\cdot\ldots\cdot l_{n}\langle v_{n}\rangle]\kern 5.0pt\text{ implies}

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,v:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\gamma\cdot l(L)\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto l_{1}\langle v_{1}\rangle\cdot l_{2}\langle v_{2}\rangle\cdot\ldots\cdot l_{n}\langle v_{n}\rangle\cdot l\langle v\rangle]\kern 5.0pt.

By inversion of the premise, we know that
\Lambda=\Lambda^{\prime},v_{1}:L_{1}
and
\gamma=L_{1}\cdot\gamma^{\prime}
in order to type
v_{1}
with some type
L_{1}
.
Thus, we can apply
RT-Queue
to our goal and then apply the induction hypothesis with
\Lambda=\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\gamma=\gamma^{\prime}
to conclude the proof.

\square

Next, we show that the queue types reflect what is in the queues of runtime configurations.

Lemma 19

If
\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::l_{1}(L_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot l_{k}(L_{k})\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto l^{\prime}_{1}\langle v^{\prime}_{1}\rangle,\ldots,l^{\prime}_{n}\langle v^{\prime}_{n}\rangle]
,
then
k=n
and,
for all
1\leq i\leq k
,
l^{\prime}_{i}=l_{i}
and
v^{\prime}_{i}:L_{i}
.

Proof

We do an induction on the depth of the typing derivation.

For the induction base, we have the following typing derivation:

\frac{}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\varepsilon\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{s}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\varepsilon}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-EmptyQueue} }

It is obvious that
k=0=n
and there are no messages to consider.

For the induction step, we have the following typing derivation:

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::l_{1}(L_{1})\cdot l_{2}(L_{2})\cdot\ldots l_{k}(L_{k})\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto l^{\prime}_{2}\langle v^{\prime}_{2}\rangle\cdot\ldots l^{\prime}_{n}\langle v^{\prime}_{n}\rangle]}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,v^{\prime}_{1}:L_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::l_{1}(L_{1})\cdot l_{2}(L_{2})\cdot\ldots l_{k}(L_{k})\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto l_{1}\langle v^{\prime}_{1}\rangle\cdot l^{\prime}_{2}\langle v^{\prime}_{2}\rangle\cdot\ldots l^{\prime}_{n}\langle v^{\prime}_{n}\rangle]}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{right={RT-Queue} }

Let us first consider the length of the queue type and the queue:
by induction hypothesis, we know that
n-1=k-1
and, thus,
k=n
.
Second, let us consider the labels and payload types.
For
i=1
, the typing rule requires the labels to match and
v^{\prime}_{1}:L_{1}
is required in the typing context.
For
i>1
, the induction hypothesis applies.

\square

We also provided typing context reductions.
Here, we show that these actually preserve reachability for the CSM associated with a session.

Lemma 20
(Typing reductions preserve reachability)

Let
\Lambda=\hat{\Lambda},\{\Lambda_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}

be a typing context
and
\Omega=\hat{\Omega},\{\Omega_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}

be a queue typing context with a set of sessions
\mathcal{S}
.
Assume that

•

\hat{\Lambda},\{\Lambda_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega},\{\Omega_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\hat{\Lambda}^{\prime},\{\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}^{\prime},\{\Omega^{\prime}_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}
, and

•

for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
, it holds that
there is
(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})
such that

\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}

and

\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}

Then, for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
, it holds that
there is
(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})
such that

\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q^{\prime}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}

and

\Omega^{\prime}_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}
.

Proof

We do inversion on

\hat{\Lambda},\{\Lambda_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega},\{\Omega_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\hat{\Lambda}^{\prime},\{\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}^{\prime},\{\Omega^{\prime}_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}
,
yielding two cases.

First, we have

\par\frac{q\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!l(L)}q^{\prime}}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q,\bar{\Lambda}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\gamma,\bar{\Omega}\longrightarrow s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q^{\prime},\bar{\Lambda}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\gamma\cdot l(L),\bar{\Omega}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={TR-$\IntCh$} }\par

for some
s
,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
.
For every
s^{\prime}\neq s
, the claim trivially holds.
For
s
,
the changes to
s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]
and
s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]
mimic the semantics of the CSM while the premise

q\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!l(L)}q^{\prime}

ensures that such a transition is possible.

For the second case where we have

\par\frac{q\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}?l\langle L\rangle}q^{\prime}}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q,\bar{\Lambda}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]::l(L)\cdot\gamma,\bar{\Omega}\longrightarrow s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q^{\prime},\bar{\Lambda}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]::\gamma,\bar{\Omega}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={TR-$\ExtCh$} }\par

the reasoning is analogous but mimics the receive case of the CSM semantics.

\square

With the substitution lemma, we prove that substituting a variable by a value with the same type preserves typability in our type system.

Lemma 21
(Substitution Lemma)

Let
R
be a well-annotated runtime configuration.
For all
L
,
if it holds that

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,x:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R
,
then

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,v:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R[v/x]\kern 5.0pt.

Proof

We do an induction on the depth of the typing derivation and do a case analysis on the last applied rule of the derivation.

For the induction base, we consider both rules with depth
0
and show that there is no
R^{\prime}
such that
R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
.
For both
RT-
\mathbf{0}
and
RT-EmptyQueue
, the second typing context is empty, which contradicts our assumption that
x:L
or
v:L
.

For the induction step, the induction hypothesis yields that the claim holds for typing derivations of smaller depth.

•

RT-
\mathsf{Q}
:

We have that

\frac{\Theta(\mathsf{Q})=L_{1},\dots,L_{n}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}c_{1}:L_{1},\ldots,c_{i-1}:L_{i-1},x:L_{i},c_{i+1}:L_{i+1},\ldots c_{n}:L_{n}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathsf{Q}[\vec{c}]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\mathsf{Q}$} }

It is straightforward that we need to show precisely the same premise for the desired typing derivation:

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}c_{1}:L_{1},\ldots,c_{i-1}:L_{i-1},v:L_{i},c_{i+1}:L_{i+1},\ldots,c_{n}:L_{n}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\mathsf{Q}[\vec{c}])[v/x]\kern 5.0pt.

•

RT-end
: We have two cases.

First, we have

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R\hskip 9.24994pt\operatorname{end}(q)}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}x:q,\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-end} }

We show that

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R[v/x]\hskip 9.24994pt\operatorname{end}(q)}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}v:q,\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R[v/x]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-end} }

For the first typing derivation, we have
x:q,\Lambda
as typing context.
By the fact that
\Lambda
is a typing context (and no syntactic typing context),

x
does not occur in
\Lambda
.
By inversion, we have

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R
.
Thus,
x
cannot occur in
R
.
If it did,
R
could only be typed with a typing context with
x
, which does not occur in
\Lambda
, given by contraposition of

Lemma

15
.
Hence
R=R[v/x]
and, thus, both premises coincide.

Second, we have

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,x:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R\hskip 9.24994pt\operatorname{end}(q)}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}c:q,\Lambda,x:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-end} }

We show that

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,v:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R[v/x]\hskip 9.24994pt\operatorname{end}(q)}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}c:q,\Lambda,v:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R[v/x]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-end} }

By inversion of the first typing derivation, we know that both premises hold.
The second premise is the same for both derivations.
For the first premise, the induction hypothesis applies.

•

RT-
\IntCh
:

Here, we do a case analysis if
x=c
,
x=c_{k}
for some
k\in I
or neither of both.

For the last case, we can apply inversion and the induction hypothesis applies to all cases of the right premise.

For
x=c
, the second premise follows from inversion and the induction hypothesis, instantiated with
L=q_{i}
.

We consider the case for
x=c_{k}
in more detail.

We have

\frac{\delta(q)\supseteq\{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!l_{i}(L_{i}),q_{i})\mid i\in I\}\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall i\in I\setminus\{k\}.\,}\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,c:q_{i},x:L,\{c_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I\setminus\{i,k\}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{i}\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,c:q_{i},\{c_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I\setminus\{k\}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{k}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,c:q,x:L,\{c_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I\setminus\{k\}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\IntCh_{i\in I}c[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]!l_{i}\langle c_{i}\rangle\,.\,P_{i}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\IntCh$} }

By inversion, we obtain all premises.

We show that

\frac{\delta(q)\supseteq\{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!l_{i}(L_{i}),q_{i})\mid i\in I\}\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall i\in I\setminus\{k\}.\,}\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,c:q_{i},v:L,\{c_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I\setminus\{i,k\}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{i}[v/x]\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,c:q_{i},\{c_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I\setminus\{k\}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{k}[v/x]}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,c:q,v:L,\{c_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I\setminus\{k\}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\IntCh_{i\in I}c[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]!l_{i}\langle c_{i}\rangle\,.\,P_{i})[v/x]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\IntCh$} }

The first premise is the same.
The second premise, for every
i\in I\setminus\{k\}
, follows by the induction hypothesis.
For the third premise, we claim that
x
cannot occur in
P_{k}
.
In the conclusion of the first typing derivation, we have the typing context

\Lambda,c:q,x:L,\{c_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I\setminus\{k\}}
.
Thus, by assumption that each element has at most one type in a typing context, we know that
x
cannot occur in

\Lambda,c:q,\{c_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I\setminus\{k\}}
.
With
Lemma

15
,
x
cannot occur in
P_{k}
.
Thus,
P_{k}[v/x]=P_{k}
and the third premise in both typing derivations coincide, concluding this case.

•

RT-
\ExtCh
:

We have

\frac{\delta(q)=\{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}?l_{i}(L_{i}),q_{i})\mid i\in I\}\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall i\in I.\,}\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,y_{i}:L_{i},c:q_{i}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{i}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,c:q\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\ExtCh_{i\in I}c[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]?l_{i}(y_{i})\,.\,P_{i}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\ExtCh$} }

We do a case analysis if
x=c
or not.

If not, the claim follows by inversion and induction hypothesis for the second premise.

If
x=c
, there is
x:q_{i}
in the second premise to type
P_{i}[v/x]
.
The existence of such a typing derivation follows from inversion and induction hypothesis when instantiated with
L=q_{i}
.

•

RT-
\parallel
:

There are two symmetric cases.
We only consider one of both.
For this, we have

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},x:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},x:L,\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}\parallel R_{2}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

We show that there is a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},v:L,\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(R_{1}\parallel R_{2})[v/x]

By our assumption that typing contexts have at most one type per element,
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}
cannot share any names.
By inversion,
we have

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}
.
Thus, by
Lemma

15
,
x
cannot occur in
R_{2}
.
Hence,
(R_{1}\parallel R_{2})[v/x]=R_{1}[v/x]\parallel R_{2}
.
We claim the following typing derivation exists:

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},v:L,\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},v:L,\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}[v/x]\parallel R_{2}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

The second premise is the same as in the original typing derivation.
The first premise can be obtained by inversion on the original typing derivation and applying the induction hypothesis.

•

RT-
\upnu
:

We have a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,x:L,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R\kern 5.0pt.

This case follows easily from inversion and applying the induction hypothesis to obtain a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,v:L,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R[v/x]\kern 5.0pt.

•

RT-Queue
:

We have a typing derivation for

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto\vec{m}]}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,v^{\prime}:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::l(L)\cdot\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto l\langle v^{\prime}\rangle\cdot\vec{m}]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-Queue} }

We do a case analysis if
x=v^{\prime}
or not.
If so, the same typing derivation can simply be re-used as
v^{\prime}:L
also disappears in the original typing derivation.
If not, the claim follows by inversion and application of the induction hypothesis.

This concludes the proof of the substitution lemma.

\square

Now, we turn to the main result about our type system:
subject reduction
.
In short, if there is a runtime configuration with a typing derivation that can take a step, then the typing contexts can also take a step and can be used to type the new runtime configuration.

Note that
Theorem

5.1
in the main text follows from
Theorem

0.C.1
.

Theorem 0.C.1
(Subject Reduction)

Let
R
be a well-annotated runtime configuration with a set of active sessions
\mathcal{S}
.
If

(1)

\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathcal{D}:\Theta
,

(2)

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R
with

\Lambda=\hat{\Lambda},\{\Lambda_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}

and

\Omega=\hat{\Omega},\{\Omega_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}
,

(3)

for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
, it holds that
there is
(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})
such that

\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}

and

\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}

(4)

R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
,

then there exist

\Lambda^{\prime}
and

\Omega^{\prime}

with

\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega^{\prime}

such that

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R^{\prime}
.

Proof

We do an induction on the depth of the typing derivation

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R

and do a case analysis on the last applied rule of the derivation.

For the induction base, we consider both rules with depth
0
and show that there is no
R^{\prime}
such that
R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
.

•

RT-
\mathbf{0}
:

It is trivial that
\mathbf{0}
cannot reduce.

•

RT-EmptyQueue
:

In this case
R=s\blacktriangleright\varepsilon
for which none of the reduction rules apply.

For the induction step, the induction hypothesis yields that the claim holds for typing derivations of smaller depth.
We do a case analysis on the typing rule that was applied last.

•

RT-
\mathsf{Q}
:

We have that

\frac{\Theta(\mathsf{Q})=L_{1},\dots,L_{n}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}c_{1}:L_{1},\dots,c_{n}:L_{n}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathsf{Q}[\vec{c}]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\mathsf{Q}$} }

Thus, we know that
R=\mathsf{Q}[\vec{c}]
.
However, none of the reduction rules apply, contradicting
(4)
.

•

RT-end
:

We have that

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R\hskip 9.24994pt\operatorname{end}(q)}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}c:q,\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-end} }

We have to show that

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\operatorname{end}(q)}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}c:q,\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R^{\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-end} }

The second premise

\operatorname{end}(q)

is the same for both.
The first premise follows by the induction hypothesis, which also gives

\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega^{\prime}
, concluding this case.

•

RT-
\IntCh
:

Inversion on the typing derivation yields that

R=\IntCh_{i\in I}s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]!l_{i}\langle v_{i}\rangle\,.\,P_{i}
.
None of the reduction rules apply and, thus, there is no
R^{\prime}
with
R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
, contradicting
(4)
.

•

RT-
\ExtCh
:

Inversion on the typing derivation yields that

R=\ExtCh_{i\in I}s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]?l_{i}(L_{i})\,.\,P_{i}
.
None of the reduction rules apply and, thus, there is no
R^{\prime}
with
R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
, contradicting
(4)
.

•

RT-
\parallel
:

We do inversion on the reduction
(4)
.

–

RR-
\mathsf{Q}
:

We have

\frac{\frac{\Theta(\mathsf{Q})=\vec{L}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\vec{c}:\vec{L}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathsf{Q}[\vec{c}]\hskip 8.04996pt}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{left={RT-$\mathsf{Q}$} }\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\vec{c}:\vec{L},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathsf{Q}[\vec{c}]\parallel R_{2}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

By inversion on
(4)
, we have

\frac{\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{Q},\vec{c})\parallel R_{2}\longrightarrow R^{\prime}}{\mathsf{Q}[\vec{c}]\parallel R_{2}\longrightarrow R^{\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RR-$\mathsf{Q}$} }

By assumption that
\mathsf{Q}
is defined in
\mathcal{D}
and by definition of
\mathcal{D}
, we have that

\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{1};(\mathsf{Q}[\vec{x}]=P);\mathcal{D}_{2}

for some
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
.

We claim there is a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\vec{c}:\vec{L}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]

(1)
states that
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathcal{D}:\Theta
.
Inversion on

(1)
for
\lvert\mathcal{D}_{1}\rvert
times yields

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\vec{x}:\vec{L}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P\kern 5.0pt.

We obtain a typing derivation after
\lvert\vec{x}\rvert
applications of the substitution lemma (
Lemma

21
).

We do a case analysis on the structure of
P
and simultaneously if
R^{\prime}=\mathbf{err}
for second case.

*

P=\IntCh_{i\in I}x[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]!l_{i}\langle x_{i}\rangle\,.\,P_{i}
:

Let us rewrite the typing context:

\vec{c}:\vec{L}=(x:q,\{x_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]
.
Without loss of generality, let
x[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]=s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]
.
Then
R^{\prime}=\lceil P_{k}[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\rceil\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\vec{m}\cdot l_{k}\langle v_{k}\rangle]

for some
k\in I
.
We show there is a typing derivation for any
k\in I
:

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q_{k},\{x_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I\setminus\{k\}},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\lceil P_{k}[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\rceil\hskip 8.19447pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(c_{k}:L_{k})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}],\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\cdot l_{k}(L_{k}[\vec{c}/\vec{x}])\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\vec{m}\cdot l_{k}\langle v_{k}\rangle]}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q_{k},\{x_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}],\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\cdot l_{k}(L_{k})\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R^{\prime}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

By inversion on

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q,\{x_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\IntCh_{i\in I}x[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]!l_{i}\langle x_{i}\rangle\,.\,P_{i})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\kern 5.0pt,

we get

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q_{i},\{x_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I\setminus\{i\}},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{i}[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]

for every
i\in I
.
Instantiating
i=k
and
applying
Lemma

16
yields the desired premise:

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q_{i},\{x_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I\setminus\{i\}},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\lceil P_{i}[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\rceil

We show there is a typing derivation

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\vec{m}]}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(c_{k}:L_{k})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}],\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\cdot l_{k}(L_{k}[\vec{c}/\vec{x}])\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\vec{m}\cdot l_{k}\langle v_{k}\rangle]}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{right={RT-Queue} }

By inversion on
R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
, we have that

R_{2}=s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\vec{m}]
.
By inversion on

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}
,
we obtain

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\vec{m}]

which is the desired premise.

It is straightforward that there is a typing context reduction for the corresponding typing contexts, using
TR-
\IntCh
.

*

P=\ExtCh_{i\in I}x[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]?l_{i}(y_{i})\,.\,P_{i}
and
R^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{err}
:

Let us rewrite the typing context:

\vec{c}:\vec{L}=(x:q,\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]
.
Without loss of generality, let
x[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]=s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]
.
Then

R^{\prime}=\lceil P_{k}[v_{k}/y_{k}]\rceil\parallel s\blacktriangleright[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\mapsto\vec{m}]

and

R_{2}=s\blacktriangleright[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\mapsto l_{k}\langle v_{k}\rangle\cdot\vec{m}]
.
We show there is a typing derivation for any
k\in I
:

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}v_{k}:L_{k},(x:q_{k},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\lceil P_{k}[\vec{c}/\vec{x}][v_{k}/y_{k}]\rceil\hskip 7.3612pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\vec{m}]}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q_{k},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}],\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\lceil P_{k}[\vec{c}/\vec{x}][v_{k}/y_{k}]\rceil\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\vec{m}]}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

By inversion on

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q,\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\ExtCh_{i\in I}x[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]?l_{i}(y_{i})\,.\,P_{i})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]
,
we get

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q_{i},y_{i}:L_{i},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{i}[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]

for every
i\in I
.
Instantiating
i=k
and applying
Lemma

16
yields the desired premise:

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}v_{k}:L_{k},(x:q_{k},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\lceil P_{k}[\vec{c}/\vec{x}][v_{k}/y_{k}]\rceil

The second premise is obtained by inversion on

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}v_{k}:L_{k},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}_{k}]::l_{k}(L_{k})\cdot\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\mapsto l_{k}\langle v_{k}\rangle\cdot\vec{m}]\kern 5.0pt.

It is straightforward that there is a typing context reduction for the corresponding typing contexts, using
TR-
\ExtCh
.

*

P=\ExtCh_{i\in I}x[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]?l_{i}(y_{i})\,.\,P_{i}
and
R^{\prime}=\mathbf{err}
:

Let us rewrite the typing context:

\vec{c}:\vec{L}=(x:q,\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]
.
Without loss of generality, let
x[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]=s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]
.
Then, by inversion on
R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
, we have

R_{2}=s\blacktriangleright\sigma
and
for every
i\in I
,

\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})=l\langle\_\rangle\cdot\vec{m}
and
l_{i}\neq l
.
We claim that there is no typing derivation

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q,\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}],\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\ExtCh_{i\in I}x[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]?l_{i}(y_{i})\,.\,P_{i})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma\kern 5.0pt.

By inversion, such a typing derivation must have the following shape:

\frac{\frac{\delta(q)=\{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\triangleleft}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}?l_{i}(L_{i}),q_{i})\mid i\in I\}\hskip 8.04996pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall i\in I.\,}\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q_{i},\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}],y_{i}:L_{i},\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{i}\hskip 8.04996pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q,\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\ExtCh_{i\in I}x[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]?l_{i}(y_{i})\,.\,P_{i})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\ExtCh$} }\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}(x:q,\Lambda_{1})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}],\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\ExtCh_{i\in I}x[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]?l_{i}(y_{i})\,.\,P_{i})[\vec{c}/\vec{x}]\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

Let us rewrite the typing and queue typing context:

\displaystyle(x:q,\Theta)[\vec{c}/\vec{x}],\Lambda_{2}

\displaystyle=\hat{\Lambda},\Lambda_{s}

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}

\displaystyle=\hat{\Omega},\Omega_{s}

By assumption, we know that
there is

(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})
such that

\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}

and

\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}
.
Recall the condition on the reduction semantics:

\forall i\in I.\,\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})=l\langle\_\rangle\cdot\vec{m}\text{ and }l_{i}\neq l
.
Thus, with
Lemma

19
, it follows that, for all
i\in I
,

\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})=l^{\prime}_{i}(\_)\cdot\_

with
l^{\prime}_{i}\neq l_{i}
.
For the CSM
\mathcal{A}
, this entails that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
expects to receive a message from a set of other participants, ranged over by
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
, but the first message in each channel does not match.
This yields a contradiction to feasible eventual reception:
there has been at least one send event to
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
and there is no matching receive event yet;
because
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
will never proceed, no matching receive event can ever happen.

–

RR-ctx
:

For the context rule, two cases apply:

R\parallel\mathbb{C}

or

\mathbb{C}\parallel R
.
Both cases can be proven analogous, which is why we only prove the first.
We have that

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{1}\parallel R_{2}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

and we want to show that

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega^{\prime}_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R_{2}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda^{\prime}_{1},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega^{\prime}_{1},\Omega_{2}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R^{\prime}_{1}\parallel R_{2}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

The second premise is trivially satisfied.
The first premise follows from the induction hypothesis, which also yields that

\Lambda_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega^{\prime}_{1}
.
We can apply

Lemma

12

to obtain

\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}_{1},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega^{\prime}_{1},\Omega_{2}
, concluding this case.

–

RR-out
:

With three inversions on the typing derivation, we have a typing derivation with the following shape for

R=\IntCh_{i\in I}s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]!l_{i}\langle v_{i}\rangle\,.\,P_{i}\parallel{\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\vec{m}]}
:

\frac{\frac{\delta(q)\supseteq\{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!l_{i}(L_{i}),q_{i})\mid i\in I\}\enskip{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall i\in I.\,}\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Lambda}_{1},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q_{i},\{v_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I\setminus\{i\}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{i}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Lambda}_{1},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q,\{v_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\IntCh_{i\in
