v_{i}\rangle\,.\,P_{i}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right={RT-$\IntCh$} }\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\vdots}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto\vec{m}]}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right={RT-Queue} }}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Lambda}_{1},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q,\{v_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

By inversion, we obtain all premises.
We show that

\frac{\frac{\vdots}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Lambda}_{1},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q_{k},\{v_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I\setminus\{k\}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\lceil P_{k}\rceil}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{right={RT-$\IntCh$} }\hskip 7.3612pt\frac{\vdots}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{2},v_{k}:L_{k}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\cdot l_{k}\langle L_{k}\rangle\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\vec{m}\cdot l_{k}\langle v_{k}\rangle]}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{right={RT-Queue} }}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Lambda}_{1},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q_{k},\{v_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I\setminus\{k\}},v_{k}:L_{k},\Lambda_{2}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega_{1},\hat{\Omega}_{2},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k}]::\gamma\cdot l_{k}\langle L_{k}\rangle\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R^{\prime}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{right={RT-$\parallel$} }

By assumption, we have a typing derivation for

Let us rewrite the typing and queue typing context:

\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}_{1},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q,\Lambda_{2}

\displaystyle=\hat{\Lambda},\Lambda_{s}

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}

\displaystyle=\hat{\Omega},\Omega_{s}

By assumption, we know that
there is

(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})
such that

\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}

and

\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}
.
Recall the condition on the reduction semantics:

\forall i\in I.\,\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})=l\langle\_\rangle\cdot\vec{m}\text{ and }l_{i}\neq l
.
Thus, with
Lemma

19
, it follows that, for all
i\in I
,

\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})=l^{\prime}_{i}(\_)\cdot\_

with
l^{\prime}_{i}\neq l_{i}
.
For the CSM
\mathcal{A}
, this entails that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
expects to receive a message from a set of other participants, ranged over by
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
, but the first message in each channel does not match.
Thus, none of them will ever be received.
This yields a contradiction to feasible eventual reception:
there has been at least one send event to
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
and there is no matching receive event yet;
because
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
will never proceed, no matching receive event can ever happen.

•

\upnu
:

By inversion on the typing derivation, there is a typing derivation

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R\kern 5.0pt.

We do inversion on
(4)
, yielding two reduction rules that apply.

–

RR-ctx
:

We have

\frac{(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})\hskip 9.24994pt\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\upnu$} }

By inversion, we obtain all premises.
We show that

\frac{(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})\hskip 9.24994pt\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Omega^{\prime}_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R^{\prime}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R^{\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\upnu$} }

The first premise is the same as for the original typing derivation.
We know that

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R
.
With the induction hypothesis,
we get

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R^{\prime}\text{ and }\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega_{s}\longrightarrow\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}_{s}\kern 5.0pt.

The first fact proves the last premise for the new typing derivation.
For the remaining ones, we apply
Lemma

20
, which yields that
there is
(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})
such that

\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q^{\prime}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}

and

\Omega^{\prime}_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}
.
These are precisely the remaining premises for the new typing derivation.
It is obvious that there is a reduction for the typing contexts, which concludes this case.

–

RR-err2
:

We have a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma

and know

\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\neq\varepsilon\text{ for some }{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
.
We do inversion on the typing derivation:

\frac{\frac{\vdots}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\Lambda_{s}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\Omega_{s}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-Queue} }\hskip 9.24994pt(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})\hskip 9.24994pt\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-$\upnu$} }

By definition of
\Lambda_{s}
, there is a type
s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q
for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}
.
There is a typing derivation for

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma\kern 5.0pt.

The only applicable typing rules (in the whole derivation) are

RT-end
,

RT-EmptyQueue
, and

RT-Queue
.
By our assumption that there is a strict partial order for the CSMs in our system,
s[\hbox{-}]
does not appear in
\sigma
.

Thus,
RT-end
needs to be applied to reduce the typing context

\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}

to only contain
\Lambda
, which can then be used to type the queue with
RT-Queue
.
The premise of
RT-end
requires that

\operatorname{end}(q)
, i.e.
q
is a final state
and has not outgoing receive transition.
This, however, entails that
(\vec{q},\xi)
is a non-final configuration where all participants are in final states
and the channels are not empty, yielding a deadlock.
This contradicts the fact that
\mathcal{A}
is deadlock-free, concluding this case.

•

RT-Queue
:

We have the typing derivation

\frac{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto\vec{m}]}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda,v:L\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega,s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::l(L)\cdot\gamma\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto l\langle v\rangle\cdot\vec{m}]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{right={RT-Queue} }

However, there is not
R^{\prime}
such that

s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto l\langle v\rangle\cdot\vec{m}]\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
, contradicting
(4)
.

This concludes the proof of subject reduction.

\square

From subject reduction,
type safety
follows:
if a process can be typed, any runtime configuration that can be reached from this process cannot contain an error.
Note that
Corollary

1
in the main text follows from
Corollary

3
.

Corollary 3
(Type Safety)

Assume that

\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\mathcal{D}:\Theta

and

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P
for some well-annotated process
P
.
If

\lceil P\rceil\longrightarrow^{*}R
,
then,
R\neq\mathbf{err}
.

Proof

From
Lemma

16
, we know that

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\lceil P\rceil
.
By definition
\longrightarrow^{*}\coloneq\{\longrightarrow^{k}\mid k\geq 0\}
.
We prove a stronger claim:
For all
k\geq 0
,
if

\lceil P\rceil\longrightarrow^{k}R
,
then,

•

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Omega\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;R
with

\Lambda=\hat{\Lambda},\{\Lambda_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}

and

\Omega=\hat{\Omega},\{\Omega_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}
, and

•

for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
, it holds that
there is
(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})
such that

\Lambda_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:q\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}

and

\Omega_{s}=\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}

This claim entails that
R\neq\mathbf{err}
because
\mathbf{err}
cannot be typed but
R
can be typed.

We prove the claim by induction on
k
.

For
k=0
, the claim trivially follows because both the typing and queue typing context is empty, trivially satisfying the conditions.

For the induction step,
we have
\lceil P\rceil\longrightarrow^{k}R
, the claim holds for
R
, and
R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
.
With Subject Reduction (
Theorem

0.C.1
), we proved precisely what we need to show for
R^{\prime}
.

\square

Subject reduction shows that any step of a runtime configuration can be mimicked by the typing contexts and these can be used to type the new runtime configuration.
Since
\mathbf{err}
cannot be typed, this shows that a typed runtime configuration can never reduce to
\mathbf{err}
, yielding type safety.
While this is a safety property,
session fidelity
deals with progress.
Roughly speaking, if the typing contexts can take a step, then the runtime configuration can also take a step.
In most MST frameworks, this can only be proven in the presence of a single session.
Thus, we define the following restriction of our type system.

Definition 32

We define
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;
to be
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;
but without the rules
PT-
\upnu
and
RT-
\upnu
.
Using this, we define
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;
for processes as follows:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}.\,}\forall c:q\in\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}.\,\operatorname{end}(q)\quad{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}.\,}\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\operatorname{init}(\mathcal{A}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,({\textstyle\prod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\quad\textsc{right={PT-$\upnu$}'}

We also define
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;
for runtime configurations:

\frac{(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})\quad{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}.\,}\forall c:q^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}.\,\operatorname{end}(q^{\prime})\quad{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}.\,}\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\quad\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}},\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,({\textstyle\prod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma}\quad\textsc{right={RT-$\upnu$}'}

If

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}},\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,({\textstyle\prod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma

holds, we know that we can obtain the premises by inversion.
For conciseness, we use the following notation
to refer to the CSM configuration
(\vec{q},\xi)
:

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}},\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\overset{(\vec{q},\xi)}{\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;}(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,({\textstyle\prod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma\kern 5.0pt.

Proposition 3

Let
P
be a process,
R
be a runtime configuration and assume

\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;\mathcal{D}:\Theta
.
If

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,P
,
then
P
is restriction-free.

If

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R
,
then
R
is restriction-free.

Intuitively,
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;
allows us to have one restriction with CSM
\mathcal{A}
and requires that all different participants of
\mathcal{A}
are played by different processes in parallel.
As argued in the main text, these are standard restrictions for session fidelity (and deadlock freedom).

As for
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;
, we show a correspondence between processes and runtime configurations for
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;
and
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;
.

Lemma 22

Let
P
be a well-annotated process.

If

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;P
,
then

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;\lceil P\rceil
.
If

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;P
,
then

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;\lceil P\rceil
.

Proof

We prove the claim
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;
by induction on the structure of
P
, as for

Lemma

16
.
For all except
P=P_{1}\parallel P_{2}
and
P=(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,P^{\prime}
, it holds that

\lceil P\rceil=P
.
For all typing rules that processes and runtime configurations share, the queue typing context is not changed in the respective runtime configuration typing rule.
Thus,

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\lceil P\rceil
.
For
P=P_{1}\parallel P_{2}
, the claim follows directly by induction hypothesis.
Since
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;
has no rule for restriction, we do not need to consider
P=(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,P^{\prime}
.

In contrast,
\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;
only applies to
P
with shape

(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,({\textstyle\prod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma
,
so we only need to consider such processes.
As for

Lemma

16
,
we can pick the initial states and empty channels.
The remaining premises follow from the first claim.

\square

Note that
Theorem

5.2
in the main text is a consequence of
Theorem

0.C.2
.

Theorem 0.C.2
(Session fidelity with sink-final FSMs)

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a deadlock-free CSM that satisfies feasible eventual reception and,
for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}
,

\mathcal{A}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
is sink-final.
Let
R
be a runtime configuration. We assume that

(1)

\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;\mathcal{D}:\Theta
,

(2)

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\overset{(\vec{q},\xi)}{\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;}(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R
, and

(3)

(\vec{q},\xi)\rightarrow(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})

for some

\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime\prime}
and
\xi^{\prime\prime}
.

Then, there is

(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})

with

(\vec{q},\xi)\rightarrow(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})

and

R^{\prime}
with
R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
such that

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\overset{(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime}\negthinspace,\,\xi^{\prime})}{\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;}(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,R^{\prime}
.

Proof

By assumption, we know that

(\vec{q},\xi)\xrightarrow{x}(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
.
We do a case analysis on the shape of
x
.

First, let
x={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!l(L)
.
We do inversion on

(2)

and rewrite

\Lambda

as

\{\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}},\Lambda^{\prime}
:

\frac{(\vec{q},\xi)\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})\quad{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}.\,}\forall c:q^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}.\,\operatorname{end}(q^{\prime})\quad{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}.\,}\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\quad\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}},\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,({\textstyle\prod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma}\quad\textsc{right={RT-$\upnu$}'}

and obtain all its premises
as well as the fact that

R=({\textstyle\prod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}}Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma
.
From the fact that
\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
has outgoing transitions, we know that it is not final, which in turn means that
\operatorname{end}(\vec{q}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}})
does not hold.
Hence,
two typing rules can apply:

PT-
\mathsf{Q}
and

PT-
\IntCh
.

We do a case analysis and show that applying
PT-
\mathsf{Q}
will eventually lead to applying
PT-
\IntCh
as well.
First, we do an inversion:

\frac{\Theta(\mathsf{Q})=\vec{L}}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\vec{c}:\vec{L}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;\mathsf{Q}[\vec{c}]}\quad\textsc{right={PT-$\mathsf{Q}$} }

Assume that we type
\mathsf{Q}[\vec{x}]
and

\mathsf{Q}[\vec{x}]=P^{\prime}
.
From

(1)
,
it follows that

\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\vec{x}:\vec{L}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;P^{\prime}
.
By assumption, we know that process definitions are guarded.
Thus,
P^{\prime}
needs to be typed with
PT-
\IntCh
.
The following arguments are very similar from now on.
In fact, there are two differences:
first, we would carry

\vec{x}:\vec{L}
around, and
second, we would apply

RR-
\mathsf{Q}
to prove that
R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
.
For conciseness, we refrain from doing so and focus on the case without the indirection through a process definition.

Thus, we consider
PT-
\IntCh
as typing rule for
Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.
By inversion, we have

\frac{\delta(q)\supseteq\{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!l_{i}(L_{i}),q_{i})\mid i\in I\}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall i\in I.\,}\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Lambda}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]:q_{i},\{c_{j}:L_{j}\}_{j\in I\setminus\{i\}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;P_{i}\quad}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Lambda}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]:q,\{c_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash}\;\IntCh_{i\in I}s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]!l_{i}\langle c_{i}\rangle\,.\,P_{i}}\quad\textsc{right={PT-$\IntCh$} }

and obtain all premises as well as the facts that

Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}=\IntCh_{i\in I}c[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}]!l_{i}\langle c_{i}\rangle\,.\,P_{i}
and

\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}=\hat{\Lambda}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},\{c_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I}
.

Because of

\delta(q)\supseteq\{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\mathbin{\color[rgb]{0,0.3,0.7}\triangleright}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!l_{i}(L_{i}),q_{i})\mid i\in I\}
,
it is possible that

l\neq l_{i}
for all
i\in I
since
\lvert I\rvert>0
by definition.
However, we know that there exists at least one label
l_{i}
that can be sent and we choose to use this for the witness

(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
.
This is precisely the reason why we cannot ensure that every possible send transition in the CSM can be followed but we can ensure that there is at least one to follow.
Let
k\in I
such that
l_{k}=l
.
We choose

R^{\prime}\coloneq({\textstyle\prod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\}}Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\parallel P_{k}\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\cdot l_{k}\langle v_{k}\rangle]

Note that

Q_{k}
is restriction by

(2)
.
Thus,
P_{k}
is restriction-free, which entails that

\lceil P_{k}\rceil=P_{k}
.
With

RR-out
and

RR-ctx
,
it is straightforward to show that

R\longrightarrow R^{\prime}
.
It remains to show that

\small\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}},\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\overset{(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime}\negthinspace,\,\xi^{\prime})}{\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;}(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,({\textstyle\prod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\}}Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\parallel P_{k}\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\cdot l_{k}\langle v_{k}\rangle]\kern 4.62497pt

We start building a typing derivation:

\frac{(a):(\vec{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})\hskip 8.19447pt(b):{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}.\,}\forall c:q^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}.\,\operatorname{end}(q^{\prime})\hskip 8.19447pt(c):{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\}.\,}\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}]:\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\hskip 8.19447pt(d):\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\hat{\Lambda}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},\{c_{i}:L_{i}\}_{i\in I\setminus\{k\}},s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]:\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;P_{k}\hskip 8.19447pt(e):\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\Lambda^{\prime},c_{k}:L_{k}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{s[{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}][{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}]::\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\}_{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\in\mathsf{Chan}_{\mathcal{A}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\mapsto\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{k})\cdot l_{k}\langle v_{k}\rangle]}{\Theta\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\{\Lambda_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}},\Lambda^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\shortmid}\emptyset\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vdash_{\tiny{SF}}}\;(\upnu s:\mathcal{A})\,({\textstyle\prod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\!\mathcal{A}}\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\}}Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})\parallel P_{k}\parallel s\blacktriangleright\sigma[({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\mapsto\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})\cdot l_{k}\langle v_{k}\rangle]}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right={RT-$\upnu$}'}

We now argue why
(a)
to
(e)
hold.

•

(a)
: This trivially holds since
(\vec{q},\xi)
was reachable and there is a transition from the latter to
(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
.

•

(b)
: This is precisely the same premise obtained by inversion of
(2)
.

•

, we can build the typing derivation with the premises obtained by inversion of

we observe that the message was appended, while the typing rules type message queues from the start.

This concludes this case.

Second, let

\frac{(a):(\vec{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\in\operatorname{reach}(\mathcal{A})\hskip before.

(a)
to

hold.

•

: This trivially holds since
(\vec{q},\xi)
was reachable and there is a transition from the latter to
(\vec{q}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{q}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
.

•

(b)
: This is precisely the same premise obtained by inversion of
(2)
.

•

(c)
: This follows from the premise obtained by inversion of
(2)
. We solely do not need the fact for
Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}

,
